    1	"""The Windows side broker of the parent owned native custody.
    2	
    3	The broker is the only creator of the native services of one round.  The
    4	parent starts it, keeps its original handle and talks to it over a private
    5	channel; the broker creates the services itself, holds their original
    6	handles and stops them again.
    7	
    8	Four properties of the broker are the reason it exists at all.
    9	
   10	* **Suspend first, adopt second.**  Every service is created with
   11	  ``CREATE_SUSPENDED``.  The broker holds the original process and thread
   12	  handle, it assigns the process to the job object of the round and only
   13	  then it resumes the thread.  A service is never started first and
   14	  adopted afterwards: a process that already ran could have broken away
   15	  from the job before the adoption.
   16	
   17	* **The job belongs to the round.**  One job object is created per round
   18	  with ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`` and without
   19	  ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK``.  The children of the services inherit
   20	  that constraint.  When the broker closes the job handle, the round is
   21	  gone.  The broker is the only owner of that handle and of the stop.
   22	
   23	* **Nothing else is inherited.**  The services get their standard output
   24	  and their standard error and the null device as their standard input.
   25	  They never get the control channel of the broker, the job handle or any
   26	  other handle of the broker.  On Windows the inheritance set is not
   27	  trusted to the default: it is passed explicitly as the handle list of
   28	  the process attributes, and every handle of that list is created as
   29	  inheritable while every other handle is not.
   30	
   31	* **Only what was created is stopped.**  The broker has no notion of a
   32	  process name, of a pid or of a record of another owner.  It stops the
   33	  handles that it holds and the job that it created, and nothing else.
   34	
   35	Nothing in this module has ever run on Windows.  Every native claim of
   36	this package therefore stays ``NOT_PROVEN``: the Windows path is written
   37	from the documented behaviour of the API and it is not a measurement.
   38	"""
   39	import collections
   40	import hashlib
   41	import json
   42	import math
   43	import os
   44	import queue
   45	import re
   46	import select
   47	import subprocess
   48	import sys
   49	import threading
   50	import time
   51	from pathlib import Path
   52	
   53	PACK_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
   54	if str(PACK_ROOT) not in sys.path:
   55	    sys.path.insert(0, str(PACK_ROOT))
   56	
   57	from tools.v0_s3_native_custody import (  # noqa: E402 - the path is set above
   58	    CHANNEL_SCHEMA, CustodyError, MAX_DESCENDANTS, MAX_FRAME_BYTES, MAX_FRAMES,
   59	    MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES, MAX_STREAM_BYTES, decode_command, validate_launch_plan)
   60	
   61	# The bounded limits of the broker.  They are internal limits of this
   62	# foundation; they are not a claim about any native Windows behaviour.
   63	WAIT_MILLISECONDS = 5000
   64	HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 120
   65	CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS = 5.0
   66	READER_JOIN_SECONDS = 2.0
   67	# The ONE cleanup budget of a round.  The stop and the cleanup of a round
   68	# share ONE absolute deadline: the deadline is generated once, at the
   69	# first stop or at the first failure of the cleanup, and it is the same
   70	# value in every layer of the cleanup.  No layer adds a budget of its own
   71	# and no layer starts a new wait with a fresh timeout.
   72	CLEANUP_BUDGET_SECONDS = 5.0
   73	# The number of the startup results that the in-flight owner of a startup
   74	# step may carry.  The channel is bounded: a startup owner that produced
   75	# more than one result at a time would be an unbounded queue.
   76	STARTUP_INFLIGHT_SLOTS = 1
   77	# The bounded wait for a startup step that did not end before the
   78	# deadline of the round.  A step that is still running after this wait
   79	# keeps its owner and the round stays unknown: the round never claims a
   80	# cleanup that it cannot prove.
   81	STARTUP_SETTLE_SECONDS = 1.0
   82	MAX_PLAN_BYTES = 64 * 1024
   83	MAX_RECEIPT_BYTES = 1024 * 1024
   84	POLL_SECONDS = 0.25
   85	
   86	# The bounded waits of the private channel.  The pipe is polled in slices
   87	# of at most 50 milliseconds, so that the cancel flag and the absolute
   88	# deadline of the round are observed even while a peer is silent.
   89	POLL_SLICE_SECONDS = 0.05
   90	PIPE_READ_BYTES = 65536
   91	# The number of the frames of the channel that is reserved for the
   92	# terminal exchange of the round.  The heartbeat may not spend them: a
   93	# round that reached its frame budget has to fail closed and it may not
   94	# reconnect and it may not renew its lease.
   95	RESERVED_TERMINAL_FRAMES = 8
   96	
   97	# The frozen environment of every child.  The variables that the existing
   98	# authorised launch wrappers remove are removed here as well, so that a
   99	# child never inherits a proxy, a user site directory or a start-up file.
  100	REMOVED_ENVIRONMENT = ('PYTHONPATH', 'PYTHONHOME', 'PYTHONSTARTUP', 'PYTHONINSPECT',
  101	                        'PYTHONUSERBASE', 'PYTHONOPTIMIZE', 'HTTP_PROXY', 'HTTPS_PROXY',
  102	                        'ALL_PROXY')
  103	
  104	
  105	class BrokerError(RuntimeError):
  106	    """A refused broker step with a stable, machine readable code."""
  107	
  108	    def __init__(self, code, detail=None):
  109	        super().__init__(code)
  110	        self.code = code
  111	        self.detail = detail
  112	
  113	
  114	# -- the one context of a round ---------------------------------------------
  115	
  116	class _RoundContext:
  117	    """The ONE finite startup and cleanup context of one round.
  118	
  119	    The context holds the four things that every layer of a round has to
  120	    share and that may never be copied from one layer into the next one.
  121	
  122	    * The **clock** of the round.  Every deadline of the round is
  123	      expressed in it, so that a deadline of one domain is never
  124	      compared with a clock of another one.
  125	
  126	    * The **absolute deadline** of the round.  It is bound once and it
  127	      may only be shortened.
  128	
  129	    * The **one cleanup deadline**.  It is published **once**, by the
  130	      first caller that enters the cleanup, and every later caller
  131	      reuses that very value: two callers that entered the cleanup at the
  132	      same moment always see the same number, and the publication is one
  133	      atomic step under the lock of the context.
  134	
  135	    * The three **one way latches** of the round: the cancel, the stop
  136	      and the unknown.  A latch that was set is never cleared, and a
  137	      late event may never overwrite the unknown of the round.
  138	
  139	    Every layer of the round holds this **one object**: the core, the
  140	    loop, the transport of the channel and the endpoints of the channel.
  141	    A layer that only copied the number of a deadline would be a layer
  142	    with a deadline of its own, and two layers with two deadlines are
  143	    two budgets.
  144	    """
  145	
  146	    def __init__(self, *, clock=None, wall=None):
  147	        self.clock = clock or time.monotonic
  148	        self.wall = wall or time.time
  149	        self._lock = threading.RLock()
  150	        self._absolute_deadline = None
  151	        self._cleanup_deadline = None
  152	        self._inflight = []
  153	        self._inflight_lock = threading.Lock()
  154	        # The one way latches of the round.
  155	        self.cancel = threading.Event()
  156	        self.stopping = threading.Event()
  157	        self.unknown = threading.Event()
  158	
  159	    # -- the deadlines ----------------------------------------------------
  160	    def bind_absolute_deadline(self, deadline):
  161	        """Bind the absolute deadline of the round.  It may only shorten."""
  162	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
  163	        if value is None:
  164	            return self._absolute_deadline
  165	        with self._lock:
  166	            if self._absolute_deadline is None or value < self._absolute_deadline:
  167	                self._absolute_deadline = value
  168	            return self._absolute_deadline
  169	
  170	    @property
  171	    def absolute_deadline(self):
  172	        with self._lock:
  173	            return self._absolute_deadline
  174	
  175	    def cleanup_deadline_value(self):
  176	        """The published cleanup deadline, or ``None`` before the first."""
  177	        with self._lock:
  178	            return self._cleanup_deadline
  179	
  180	    def cleanup_deadline(self, deadline=None):
  181	        """Publish the ONE cleanup deadline of the round, atomically.
  182	
  183	        The deadline is the earlier of the absolute deadline of the
  184	        round, the fixed cleanup budget of the round and the deadline
  185	        that the caller brought.  It is generated **once**: every later
  186	        caller receives the very same value and may never extend it and
  187	        may never replace it.
  188	        """
  189	        with self._lock:
  190	            if self._cleanup_deadline is None:
  191	                value = self.clock() + CLEANUP_BUDGET_SECONDS
  192	                if self._absolute_deadline is not None:
  193	                    value = min(value, self._absolute_deadline)
  194	                candidate = _require_deadline(deadline)
  195	                if candidate is not None:
  196	                    value = min(value, candidate)
  197	                self._cleanup_deadline = value
  198	            return self._cleanup_deadline
  199	
  200	    def remaining_cleanup(self, deadline=None):
  201	        """The seconds that are left until the cleanup deadline.
  202	
  203	        The value is recomputed on every call: a wait that already
  204	        consumed part of the budget never receives the whole budget
  205	        again.
  206	        """
  207	        value = self.cleanup_deadline(deadline)
  208	        return max(0.0, value - self.clock())
  209	
  210	    def cleanup_expired(self):
  211	        """Whether the shared cleanup deadline of the round arrived."""
  212	        with self._lock:
  213	            value = self._cleanup_deadline
  214	        if value is None:
  215	            return False
  216	        return self.clock() >= value
  217	
  218	    def remaining_absolute(self):
  219	        """The seconds that are left until the absolute deadline.
  220	
  221	        The value is recomputed on every call and it is read from the
  222	        **one** context of the round: a layer that copied the number of
  223	        the deadline would have a deadline of its own.  ``None`` means
  224	        that the round did not bind an absolute deadline at all.
  225	        """
  226	        with self._lock:
  227	            value = self._absolute_deadline
  228	        if value is None:
  229	            return None
  230	        return max(0.0, value - self.clock())
  231	
  232	    def effective_deadline(self, call=None, bound=None):
  233	        """The ONE deadline that a call of a layer really uses.
  234	
  235	        The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
  236	        layer, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
  237	        cleanup deadline of the round are one value: the earliest of
  238	        the four.
  239	
  240	        The published cleanup deadline is part of it.  A round that
  241	        entered its cleanup publishes one deadline, and every layer that
  242	        reads the context afterwards is bound by it: a transport that
  243	        still waited with the long lease of the round would outlive the
  244	        only cleanup budget of the round.
  245	
  246	        The value is **recomputed on every call**: a deadline that was
  247	        published after the call started still shortens the call that is
  248	        running.
  249	        """
  250	        value = _require_deadline(call)
  251	        for candidate in (bound, self.absolute_deadline,
  252	                          self.cleanup_deadline_value()):
  253	            if candidate is None:
  254	                continue
  255	            if value is None or candidate < value:
  256	                value = candidate
  257	        return value
  258	
  259	    # -- the one way latches ----------------------------------------------
  260	    def request_cancel(self):
  261	        """Ask for the cancellation of the round.  One way."""
  262	        self.cancel.set()
  263	
  264	    def request_stop(self):
  265	        """Ask for the stop of the round.  One way."""
  266	        self.stopping.set()
  267	
  268	    def latch_unknown(self):
  269	        """Latch the unknown state of the round.  One way.
  270	
  271	        The first caller learns that it latched the state and every later
  272	        caller learns that the state was already latched.  A late event
  273	        may never clear the latch and may never replace it.
  274	        """
  275	        if self.unknown.is_set():
  276	            return False
  277	        self.unknown.set()
  278	        return True
  279	
  280	    def is_unknown(self):
  281	        """Whether the unknown state of the round was latched."""
  282	        return self.unknown.is_set()
  283	
  284	    # -- the owners of the operations in flight ---------------------------
  285	    def register_inflight(self, owner):
  286	        """Take the ownership of one operation that is in flight."""
  287	        with self._inflight_lock:
  288	            if owner not in self._inflight:
  289	                self._inflight.append(owner)
  290	        return owner
  291	
  292	    def release_inflight(self, owner):
  293	        """Give the ownership of one operation back."""
  294	        with self._inflight_lock:
  295	            if owner in self._inflight:
  296	                self._inflight.remove(owner)
  297	
  298	    def inflight(self):
  299	        """The owners of the operations that are in flight."""
  300	        with self._inflight_lock:
  301	            return list(self._inflight)
  302	
  303	
  304	# -- the Windows primitive table -------------------------------------------
  305	
  306	def _windows_command_line(executable, arguments):
  307	    """Build the command line of one child.
  308	
  309	    The quoting is the quoting of the Microsoft C runtime: a backslash run
  310	    in front of a quote is doubled and a trailing backslash run is doubled
  311	    as well, so that a path with a backslash is never truncated by the
  312	    parser of the child.
  313	    """
  314	    parts = [_quote_windows_argument(executable)]
  315	    parts.extend(_quote_windows_argument(item) for item in arguments)
  316	    return ' '.join(parts)
  317	
  318	
  319	def _quote_windows_argument(value):
  320	    text = str(value)
  321	    if text and not any(character in text for character in ' \t"'):
  322	        return text
  323	    quoted = ['"']
  324	    backslashes = 0
  325	    for character in text:
  326	        if character == '\\':
  327	            backslashes += 1
  328	            continue
  329	        if character == '"':
  330	            quoted.append('\\' * (backslashes * 2 + 1))
  331	            quoted.append('"')
  332	            backslashes = 0
  333	            continue
  334	        if backslashes:
  335	            quoted.append('\\' * backslashes)
  336	            backslashes = 0
  337	        quoted.append(character)
  338	    quoted.append('\\' * (backslashes * 2))
  339	    quoted.append('"')
  340	    return ''.join(quoted)
  341	
  342	
  343	def _environment_block(environment):
  344	    """Build the Unicode environment block of one child."""
  345	    if environment is None:
  346	        return None
  347	    import ctypes
  348	    items = sorted(environment.items(), key=lambda item: item[0].casefold())
  349	    raw = ''.join('%s=%s\0' % (key, value) for key, value in items) + '\0'
  350	    return ctypes.create_unicode_buffer(raw)
  351	
  352	
  353	def _windows_api_types():
  354	    """The pure declaration of the Windows structures of the broker.
  355	
  356	    The declaration is built **without** a native library.  It declares
  357	    the layout of the structures and it returns them together with the
  358	    ``ctypes`` module that built them, so that the layout can be measured
  359	    and checked off Windows and so that the loaded primitive table uses
  360	    exactly this declaration.
  361	
  362	    The fixed width types are declared **explicitly**.  ``ctypes.wintypes``
  363	    is a module of the host: its ``BOOL`` is a ``c_long`` and its
  364	    ``DWORD`` is a ``c_uint``, and the size of a ``c_long`` is not the
  365	    size of a Windows ``BOOL`` on every host.  A layout that was built
  366	    from the types of the host would therefore be a layout of the host
  367	    and not the layout of Windows.  Here a ``DWORD`` and a ``BOOL`` are
  368	    always 32 bit, a ``WORD`` is always 16 bit and a handle and a
  369	    ``SIZE_T`` have the width of a pointer.
  370	
  371	    A name of a class body is resolved in the class namespace and in the
  372	    global namespace only.  A class body therefore never assigns a name
  373	    that shadows an enclosing local: ``kernel32 = kernel32`` would raise
  374	    a ``NameError`` instead of copying the value, which is why the table
  375	    of the broker is built from an explicit namespace and never from the
  376	    body of a class.
  377	    """
  378	    import ctypes
  379	
  380	    DWORD = ctypes.c_uint32
  381	    BOOL = ctypes.c_int32
  382	    WORD = ctypes.c_uint16
  383	    UINT = ctypes.c_uint32
  384	    LONG = ctypes.c_int32
  385	    ULONG = ctypes.c_uint32
  386	    HANDLE = ctypes.c_void_p
  387	    SIZE_T = ctypes.c_size_t
  388	    ULONG_PTR = ctypes.c_size_t
  389	    LPCWSTR = ctypes.c_wchar_p
  390	    LPWSTR = ctypes.c_wchar_p
  391	
  392	    class STARTUPINFOW(ctypes.Structure):
  393	        _fields_ = [
  394	            ('cb', DWORD),
  395	            ('lpReserved', LPWSTR),
  396	            ('lpDesktop', LPWSTR),
  397	            ('lpTitle', LPWSTR),
  398	            ('dwX', DWORD),
  399	            ('dwY', DWORD),
  400	            ('dwXSize', DWORD),
  401	            ('dwYSize', DWORD),
  402	            ('dwXCountChars', DWORD),
  403	            ('dwYCountChars', DWORD),
  404	            ('dwFillAttribute', DWORD),
  405	            ('dwFlags', DWORD),
  406	            ('wShowWindow', WORD),
  407	            ('cbReserved2', WORD),
  408	            ('lpReserved2', ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_byte)),
  409	            ('hStdInput', HANDLE),
  410	            ('hStdOutput', HANDLE),
  411	            ('hStdError', HANDLE),
  412	        ]
  413	
  414	    class STARTUPINFOEXW(ctypes.Structure):
  415	        _fields_ = [('StartupInfo', STARTUPINFOW),
  416	                    ('lpAttributeList', ctypes.c_void_p)]
  417	
  418	    class PROCESS_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
  419	        _fields_ = [('hProcess', HANDLE),
  420	                    ('hThread', HANDLE),
  421	                    ('dwProcessId', DWORD),
  422	                    ('dwThreadId', DWORD)]
  423	
  424	    class SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES(ctypes.Structure):
  425	        """The security attributes of one inheritable handle.
  426	
  427	        ``bInheritHandle`` is a ``BOOL`` and it is a 32 bit value: a
  428	        handle that is created with ``NULL`` security attributes can
  429	        never be inherited at all.
  430	        """
  431	        _fields_ = [('nLength', DWORD),
  432	                    ('lpSecurityDescriptor', ctypes.c_void_p),
  433	                    ('bInheritHandle', BOOL)]
  434	
  435	    class FILETIME(ctypes.Structure):
  436	        _fields_ = [('dwLowDateTime', DWORD),
  437	                    ('dwHighDateTime', DWORD)]
  438	
  439	    class OVERLAPPED(ctypes.Structure):
  440	        """The overlapped structure of one asynchronous operation.
  441	
  442	        The structure owns the event of the completion and the offset
  443	        of the operation.  It is released only after the completion of
  444	        the operation was proven.
  445	        """
  446	        _fields_ = [('Internal', SIZE_T),
  447	                    ('InternalHigh', SIZE_T),
  448	                    ('Offset', DWORD),
  449	                    ('OffsetHigh', DWORD),
  450	                    ('hEvent', HANDLE)]
  451	
  452	    class IO_COUNTERS(ctypes.Structure):
  453	        _fields_ = [('ReadOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
  454	                    ('WriteOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
  455	                    ('OtherOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
  456	                    ('ReadTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
  457	                    ('WriteTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
  458	                    ('OtherTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong)]
  459	
  460	    class JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
  461	        _fields_ = [('PerProcessUserTimeLimit', ctypes.c_longlong),
  462	                    ('PerJobUserTimeLimit', ctypes.c_longlong),
  463	                    ('LimitFlags', DWORD),
  464	                    ('MinimumWorkingSetSize', SIZE_T),
  465	                    ('MaximumWorkingSetSize', SIZE_T),
  466	                    ('ActiveProcessLimit', DWORD),
  467	                    ('Affinity', SIZE_T),
  468	                    ('PriorityClass', DWORD),
  469	                    ('SchedulingClass', DWORD)]
  470	
  471	    class JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
  472	        _fields_ = [('BasicLimitInformation', JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION),
  473	                    ('IoInfo', IO_COUNTERS),
  474	                    ('ProcessMemoryLimit', SIZE_T),
  475	                    ('JobMemoryLimit', SIZE_T),
  476	                    ('PeakProcessMemoryUsed', SIZE_T),
  477	                    ('PeakJobMemoryUsed', SIZE_T)]
  478	
  479	    class JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST(ctypes.Structure):
  480	        """The process id list of a job object.
  481	
  482	        The list is an **inline** array of ``ULONG_PTR`` and not a
  483	        pointer: the first process id is written at the offset of the
  484	        array and every other id follows it.  A structure that declared
  485	        a pointer field here would read the first id as an address and
  486	        would dereference it.
  487	        """
  488	        _fields_ = [('NumberOfAssignedProcesses', DWORD),
  489	                    ('NumberOfProcessIdsInList', DWORD),
  490	                    ('ProcessIdList', ULONG_PTR * 1)]
  491	
  492	    return {
  493	        'ctypes': ctypes,
  494	        'DWORD': DWORD,
  495	        'BOOL': BOOL,
  496	        'WORD': WORD,
  497	        'UINT': UINT,
  498	        'LONG': LONG,
  499	        'ULONG': ULONG,
  500	        'HANDLE': HANDLE,
  501	        'SIZE_T': SIZE_T,
  502	        'ULONG_PTR': ULONG_PTR,
  503	        'LPCWSTR': LPCWSTR,
  504	        'LPWSTR': LPWSTR,
  505	        'STARTUPINFOW': STARTUPINFOW,
  506	        'STARTUPINFOEXW': STARTUPINFOEXW,
  507	        'PROCESS_INFORMATION': PROCESS_INFORMATION,
  508	        'SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES': SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES,
  509	        'FILETIME': FILETIME,
  510	        'OVERLAPPED': OVERLAPPED,
  511	        'IO_COUNTERS': IO_COUNTERS,
  512	        'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION': JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
  513	        'JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION': JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
  514	        'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST': JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST,
  515	    }
  516	
  517	
  518	def _declare_windows_primitives(kernel32, types):
  519	    """Declare the prototypes of the Windows primitives of the broker.
  520	
  521	    Every prototype is explicit.  A handle is a ``HANDLE``, a pointer is
  522	    an explicit ``ctypes`` pointer, a size is a ``SIZE_T`` and every
  523	    value that Windows defines as a 32 bit number is a ``DWORD`` or a
  524	    ``BOOL``.  A primitive without a declared prototype takes its
  525	    arguments as ``c_int`` and would truncate a 64 bit handle.
  526	
  527	    The declaration is applied to the primitive table that is handed in,
  528	    so the very same code declares the table of a real run and the table
  529	    of an offline inspection.
  530	    """
  531	    ctypes = types['ctypes']
  532	    void_p = ctypes.c_void_p
  533	    size_t = types['SIZE_T']
  534	    handle = types['HANDLE']
  535	    boolean = types['BOOL']
  536	    dword = types['DWORD']
  537	    uint = types['UINT']
  538	    ulong = types['ULONG']
  539	
  540	    kernel32.CreateJobObjectW.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']),
  541	                                          types['LPCWSTR']]
  542	    kernel32.CreateJobObjectW.restype = handle
  543	    kernel32.SetInformationJobObject.argtypes = [handle, ctypes.c_int, void_p, dword]
  544	    kernel32.SetInformationJobObject.restype = boolean
  545	    kernel32.CreateProcessW.argtypes = [types['LPCWSTR'], types['LPWSTR'],
  546	                                        ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']),
  547	                                        ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']),
  548	                                        boolean, dword, void_p, types['LPCWSTR'],
  549	                                        ctypes.POINTER(types['STARTUPINFOW']),
  550	                                        ctypes.POINTER(types['PROCESS_INFORMATION'])]
  551	    kernel32.CreateProcessW.restype = boolean
  552	    kernel32.AssignProcessToJobObject.argtypes = [handle, handle]
  553	    kernel32.AssignProcessToJobObject.restype = boolean
  554	    kernel32.ResumeThread.argtypes = [handle]
  555	    kernel32.ResumeThread.restype = dword
  556	    kernel32.TerminateProcess.argtypes = [handle, uint]
  557	    kernel32.TerminateProcess.restype = boolean
  558	    kernel32.TerminateJobObject.argtypes = [handle, uint]
  559	    kernel32.TerminateJobObject.restype = boolean
  560	    kernel32.WaitForSingleObject.argtypes = [handle, dword]
  561	    kernel32.WaitForSingleObject.restype = dword
  562	    kernel32.GetExitCodeProcess.argtypes = [handle, ctypes.POINTER(dword)]
  563	    kernel32.GetExitCodeProcess.restype = boolean
  564	    kernel32.GetProcessTimes.argtypes = [handle,
  565	                                         ctypes.POINTER(types['FILETIME']),
  566	                                         ctypes.POINTER(types['FILETIME']),
  567	                                         ctypes.POINTER(types['FILETIME']),
  568	                                         ctypes.POINTER(types['FILETIME'])]
  569	    kernel32.GetProcessTimes.restype = boolean
  570	    kernel32.QueryInformationJobObject.argtypes = [handle, ctypes.c_int, void_p,
  571	                                                   dword, ctypes.POINTER(dword)]
  572	    kernel32.QueryInformationJobObject.restype = boolean
  573	    kernel32.CloseHandle.argtypes = [handle]
  574	    kernel32.CloseHandle.restype = boolean
  575	    kernel32.CreatePipe.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(handle), ctypes.POINTER(handle),
  576	                                    ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']), dword]
  577	    kernel32.CreatePipe.restype = boolean
  578	    kernel32.ReadFile.argtypes = [handle, void_p, dword,
  579	                                  ctypes.POINTER(dword), void_p]
  580	    kernel32.ReadFile.restype = boolean
  581	    kernel32.WriteFile.argtypes = [handle, void_p, dword,
  582	                                   ctypes.POINTER(dword), void_p]
  583	    kernel32.WriteFile.restype = boolean
  584	    kernel32.SetHandleInformation.argtypes = [handle, dword, dword]
  585	    kernel32.SetHandleInformation.restype = boolean
  586	    kernel32.CreateFileW.argtypes = [types['LPCWSTR'], dword, dword,
  587	                                     ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']),
  588	                                     dword, dword, handle]
  589	    kernel32.CreateFileW.restype = handle

  591	                                                           ctypes.POINTER(size_t)]
  592	    kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList.restype = boolean
  593	    kernel32.UpdateProcThreadAttribute.argtypes = [void_p, dword, size_t, void_p,
  594	                                                   size_t, void_p, void_p]
  595	    kernel32.UpdateProcThreadAttribute.restype = boolean
  596	    kernel32.DeleteProcThreadAttributeList.argtypes = [void_p]
  597	    kernel32.DeleteProcThreadAttributeList.restype = None
  598	    # The cancellation of the private channel.  ``CancelSynchronousIo``
  599	    # cancels the synchronous operation of one *thread* and it needs the
  600	    # original handle of that thread, never a self reported identifier.
  601	    kernel32.CancelSynchronousIo.argtypes = [handle]
  602	    kernel32.CancelSynchronousIo.restype = boolean
  603	    kernel32.DuplicateHandle.argtypes = [handle, handle, handle, ctypes.POINTER(handle),
  604	                                         dword, boolean, dword]
  605	    kernel32.DuplicateHandle.restype = boolean
  606	    kernel32.GetCurrentThread.argtypes = []

  609	    kernel32.GetCurrentProcess.restype = handle

  611	
  612	class _WindowsApiTable:
  613	    """The namespace of the primitive table of the broker.
  614	
  615	    The attributes are assigned explicitly.  A class body resolves a
  616	    name that it assigns itself in the class namespace and in the global
  617	    namespace only, so a class body that copies an enclosing local into
  618	    a name of its own raises a ``NameError`` instead of copying the
  619	    value.
  620	    """
  621	
  622	    def __init__(self, *, kernel32, types):
  623	        self.kernel32 = kernel32
  624	        self.types = dict(types)
  625	        for name in ('STARTUPINFOW', 'STARTUPINFOEXW', 'PROCESS_INFORMATION',
  626	                     'SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES', 'IO_COUNTERS',
  627	                     'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION',
  628	                     'JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION',
  629	                     'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST'):
  630	            setattr(self, name, types[name])
  631	
  632	    def __repr__(self):  # pragma: no cover - a diagnostic only
  633	        return '<WindowsApiTable types=%d>' % len(self.types)
  634	
  635	
  636	def _load_windows_api():
  637	    """Load the Windows primitive table of the broker.
  638	
  639	    The load is lazy and it happens only inside the explicit native entry.
  640	    An import of this module on a platform that is not Windows never
  641	    touches ``WinDLL`` and never resolves a symbol, so the module stays
  642	    importable and testable everywhere.  An unsupported platform refuses
  643	    here, before anything is created: it is never degraded into an
  644	    unconstrained process.
  645	
  646	    The declaration of the structures and the declaration of the
  647	    prototypes are two separate functions, so that the layout and the
  648	    prototypes can be inspected without loading a native library at all.
  649	    """
  650	    if os.name != 'nt':
  651	        raise BrokerError('BROKER_PLATFORM_UNSUPPORTED')
  652	    import ctypes
  653	    kernel32 = ctypes.WinDLL('kernel32', use_last_error=True)
  654	    types = _windows_api_types()
  655	    _declare_windows_primitives(kernel32, types)
  656	    return _WindowsApiTable(kernel32=kernel32, types=types)
  657	
  658	
  659	class CreatedProcess:
  660	    """The original handles of one created process."""
  661	
  662	    __slots__ = ('process', 'thread', 'pid', 'creation_time', 'failures')
  663	
  664	    def __init__(self, process, thread, pid, creation_time, failures=()):
  665	        self.process = process
  666	        self.thread = thread
  667	        self.pid = pid
  668	        self.creation_time = creation_time
  669	        self.failures = tuple(failures)
  670	
  671	
  672	class WindowsApi:
  673	    """The typed Windows primitive façade of the broker.
  674	
  675	    Every method of this class wraps exactly one Windows API call, and the
  676	    broker never calls a Windows function itself.  That keeps the native
  677	    detail - the pointer sizes, the handle ownership and the last error -
  678	    in one place, and it lets the offline validation drive the very same
  679	    broker logic through an in-process façade of the same shape.
  680	
  681	    The declarations are pointer size safe: a handle is a
  682	    ``wintypes.HANDLE``, every pointer is an explicit ``ctypes`` pointer
  683	    and every structure declares its fields.  The last error of a failed
  684	    call is read with ``ctypes.get_last_error()``, which is only correct
  685	    because the module was loaded with ``use_last_error=True``.
  686	    """
  687	
  688	    CREATE_SUSPENDED = 0x00000004
  689	    EXTENDED_STARTUPINFO_PRESENT = 0x00080000
  690	    STARTF_USESTDHANDLES = 0x00000100
  691	    CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT = 0x00000400
  692	    JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE = 0x00002000
  693	    JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK = 0x01000000
  694	    JOB_OBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION = 9
  695	    JOB_OBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST = 3
  696	    WAIT_OBJECT_0 = 0
  697	    WAIT_TIMEOUT = 0x00000102
  698	    HANDLE_FLAG_INHERIT = 0x00000001
  699	    PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST = 0x00020002
  700	    GENERIC_READ = 0x80000000
  701	    FILE_SHARE_READ = 0x00000001
  702	    FILE_SHARE_WRITE = 0x00000002
  703	    OPEN_EXISTING = 3
  704	    FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL = 0x00000080
  705	    ERROR_MORE_DATA = 234
  706	    ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE = 109
  707	    ERROR_PIPE_NOT_CONNECTED = 233
  708	    ERROR_HANDLE_EOF = 38
  709	    ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED = 995
  710	    ERROR_NOT_FOUND = 1168
  711	    DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS = 0x00000002
  712	
  713	    # -- the ownership states of one retained handle ----------------------
  714	    #: The façade still owns the handle and nobody else may take it.
  715	    API_OWNED = 'API_OWNED'
  716	    #: The recovery of the façade has **claimed** the handle and it is
  717	    #: preparing to close it.  A handover may not take it and a second
  718	    #: recovery may not claim it again while the claim is alive.
  719	    RECOVERY_CLAIMED = 'RECOVERY_CLAIMED'
  720	    #: The handover of the handle is running: the receiver registers it
  721	    #: and the façade forgets it in one atomic step.
  722	    TRANSFER_PENDING = 'TRANSFER_PENDING'
  723	    #: The handle was handed over to its new owner.  It is not in the
  724	    #: holding table any more and it is never closed by the recovery.
  725	    TRANSFERRED = 'TRANSFERRED'
  726	    #: The handle was closed with a confirmed close.
  727	    CLOSED = 'CLOSED'
  728	
  729	    def __init__(self, table=None):
  730	        """The typed façade of the Windows primitives.
  731	
  732	        The table is the primitive table of the broker.  A table may be
  733	        handed in for the inspection of the declaration and of the
  734	        layout: the table of a real run is the one that loads the
  735	        native library, and the table of an inspection never loads one.
  736	        The code that uses the façade is the same code in both cases.
  737	        """
  738	        self._api = table if table is not None else _load_windows_api()
  739	        # The invalid value of a handle has the width of a pointer: it is
  740	        # ``-1`` as a 32 bit value and it is ``0xffffffffffffffff`` as a
  741	        # 64 bit one.  A comparison with the Python value ``-1`` would
  742	        # never match the 64 bit value.
  743	        self.invalid_handle_value = self._api.types['ctypes'].c_void_p(-1).value
  744	        # The holding table of the façade.  Every original handle that a
  745	        # successful ``CreateProcess`` produced enters this table in the
  746	        # moment of its creation, before anything else may fail.  A handle
  747	        # that was handed over to its owner leaves the table; a handle
  748	        # that could not be handed over stays in it, with its owner, its
  749	        # pid and the failures of its release.  The table is the recovery
  750	        # entry point of the façade: the core keeps the façade and it can
  751	        # therefore always reach the handles that it never received.
  752	        self._retained = {}
  753	        self._retained_order = []
  754	        # The one lock of the holding table.  Every mutation of the
  755	        # table and every handover of a handle out of it happen under
  756	        # this lock: a handle that is registered by its receiver and
  757	        # that the façade still names would have **two** owners, and a
  758	        # handle that left the façade before its receiver registered it
  759	        # would have none.
  760	        self._table_lock = threading.RLock()
  761	        # The handovers that really happened.  The list is the evidence
  762	        # of the unique owner of a handle: a handle that was handed
  763	        # over is not in the holding table any more and it is never
  764	        # closed by the recovery of the façade.
  765	        self._handovers = []
  766	        # The ONE deadline of the recovery of the façade.  It is
  767	        # published once: a second recovery of the same round reuses
  768	        # the very same value and it never opens a new budget.  It may
  769	        # only be **shortened** by a context that arrived later.
  770	        self._recovery_deadline = None
  771	        # The absolute deadline of the round, when the façade is bound to
  772	        # one.  Every wait of the façade is bounded by it: a wait that
  773	        # does not fit into the deadline of the round never starts.
  774	        self._deadline = None
  775	        # The ONE context of the round.  The façade reads the absolute
  776	        # deadline and the published cleanup deadline of the round from
  777	        # it and it never keeps a budget of its own.
  778	        self._context = None
  779	        self._clock = time.monotonic
  780	
  781	    def bind_deadline(self, deadline, clock=None):
  782	        """Bind the absolute deadline of the round to this façade.
  783	
  784	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
  785	        bound.  The waits of the façade - the wait of a release and the
  786	        wait of the recovery - are bounded by it and they never start a
  787	        wait with a fresh timeout of their own.
  788	        """
  789	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
  790	        if value is None:
  791	            return self._deadline
  792	        if clock is not None:
  793	            self._clock = clock
  794	        if self._deadline is None or value < self._deadline:
  795	            self._deadline = value
  796	        return self._deadline
  797	
  798	    def bind_context(self, context):
  799	        """Bind the ONE context of the round to this façade.
  800	
  801	        The façade reads the absolute deadline and the published
  802	        cleanup deadline of the round from the context and it never
  803	        keeps a budget of its own: a recovery that opened a fresh
  804	        budget of its own would outlive the round that it recovers.
  805	        """
  806	        if context is not None:
  807	            self._context = context
  808	        return self._context
  809	
  810	    def _effective_deadline(self, call=None):
  811	        """The ONE deadline that a wait of this façade really uses.
  812	
  813	        The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
  814	        façade, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
  815	        cleanup deadline of the round are one value: the earliest of
  816	        the four.  The value is recomputed on every call, so a deadline
  817	        that was published after the wait started still shortens it.
  818	        """
  819	        value = _require_deadline(call)
  820	        bound = self._deadline
  821	        if bound is not None and (value is None or bound < value):
  822	            value = bound
  823	        context = self._context
  824	        if context is not None:
  825	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
  826	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
  827	                value = absolute
  828	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
  829	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
  830	                value = cleanup
  831	        return value
  832	
  833	    def _wait_timeout_ms(self):
  834	        """The milliseconds that are left until the bound deadline.
  835	
  836	        A façade without a bound deadline keeps the closed wait of the
  837	        package.  A façade with one never waits longer than the deadline
  838	        of the round allows.
  839	        """
  840	        value = self._effective_deadline()
  841	        if value is None:
  842	            return WAIT_MILLISECONDS
  843	        remaining = value - self._clock()
  844	        if remaining <= 0:
  845	            return 0
  846	        return max(1, min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000)))
  847	
  848	    def _last_error(self):
  849	        """The last error of the last failing primitive of the host.
  850	
  851	        The value is read with ``ctypes.get_last_error()``, which is
  852	        only correct because the module was loaded with
  853	        ``use_last_error=True``.  A host that does not have that
  854	        function at all cannot report a last error and reports zero.
  855	        """
  856	        getter = getattr(self._api.types['ctypes'], 'get_last_error', None)
  857	        if getter is None:
  858	            return 0
  859	        return int(getter())
  860	
  861	    def _security_attributes(self, inherit):
  862	        """The security attributes of one handle.
  863	
  864	        The attributes say that the handle may be inherited.  A handle
  865	        that is created with ``NULL`` security attributes is never
  866	        inheritable, so the handle list of the process attributes would
  867	        name a handle that the child cannot receive.
  868	        """
  869	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
  870	        attributes = self._api.types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']()
  871	        attributes.nLength = ctypes.sizeof(self._api.types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES'])
  872	        attributes.lpSecurityDescriptor = None
  873	        attributes.bInheritHandle = bool(inherit)
  874	        return attributes
  875	
  876	    # -- the holding table of the original handles -------------------------
  877	    def _retain(self, handle, kind, pid=None, stage='CREATED'):
  878	        """Take one original handle into the holding table of the façade.
  879	
  880	        The table is entered in the moment of the creation of the handle
  881	        and it is left only when the handle was closed with a confirmed
  882	        close or when it was handed over to its owner.  A handle that
  883	        stays in the table is a resource that this round still owns and
  884	        it is named with its kind, its pid and its failures.
  885	        """
  886	        key = id(handle)
  887	        with self._table_lock:
  888	            if key not in self._retained:
  889	                self._retained_order.append(key)
  890	            self._retained[key] = {'handle': handle, 'kind': kind, 'pid': pid,
  891	                                   'stage': stage, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_API',
  892	                                   'state': self.API_OWNED,
  893	                                   'failures': []}
  894	            return self._retained[key]
  895	
  896	    def _forget(self, handle):
  897	        """Leave the holding table: the handle is closed or handed over."""
  898	        key = id(handle)
  899	        with self._table_lock:
  900	            if key in self._retained:
  901	                del self._retained[key]
  902	                if key in self._retained_order:
  903	                    self._retained_order.remove(key)
  904	                return True
  905	        return False
  906	
  907	    def forget_handle(self, handle):
  908	        """Leave the holding table, under the lock of the table.
  909	
  910	        The method is the public face of ``_forget``: the receiver of a
  911	        handle uses it when the façade of the primitives does not offer
  912	        the atomic handover.  A façade that offers the handover uses
  913	        that one instead.
  914	        """
  915	        return self._forget(handle)
  916	
  917	    def _note(self, handle, failure):
  918	        """Name one failure of one retained handle."""
  919	        key = id(handle)
  920	        with self._table_lock:
  921	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
  922	            if entry is not None:
  923	                entry['failures'].append(failure)
  924	            return entry
  925	
  926	    def retained_handles(self):
  927	        """The original handles that this façade still holds.
  928	
  929	        The list is a copy: a caller that changes it changes nothing of
  930	        the holding table of the façade.
  931	        """
  932	        with self._table_lock:
  933	            return [dict(self._retained[key]) for key in self._retained_order
  934	                    if key in self._retained]
  935	
  936	    def handovers(self):
  937	        """The handovers that really happened, as a copy of the log."""
  938	        with self._table_lock:
  939	            return [dict(item) for item in self._handovers]
  940	
  941	    def handover(self, entries, *, owner, register):
  942	        """Hand the named handles over to their new owner, in one step.
  943	
  944	        The receiver registers the handles and the façade forgets them
  945	        under **one** lock.  The two facts are one atomic step: a
  946	        handle that the receiver registered and that the façade still
  947	        names would have two independent owners, and the recovery of
  948	        the façade could close a handle that a live reader of the round
  949	        is reading from.  A handle that left the façade before its
  950	        receiver registered it would have no owner at all.
  951	
  952	        The handover and the recovery of the façade are **the same
  953	        ownership decision**.  A handle that the recovery has already
  954	        claimed is refused here: the recovery is preparing to close it
  955	        and a second owner may never appear.  A refused handle stays
  956	        with the façade and it is never registered with the receiver.
  957	
  958	        A registration that fails leaves the handle with the façade:
  959	        the state of the entry is restored and the handle is never
  960	        half registered and closed by two owners at the same time.
  961	
  962	        ``entries`` is the list of ``(handle, kind)`` pairs and
  963	        ``register`` receives it and registers every handle with the
  964	        receiver.  The handover is recorded, so that the evidence of the
  965	        round can name the owner that received the handle.
  966	        """
  967	        items = list(entries)
  968	        refused = []
  969	        with self._table_lock:
  970	            accepted = []
  971	            for handle, kind in items:
  972	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
  973	                if entry is not None and entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
  974	                    # The recovery of the façade owns this handle and it
  975	                    # is preparing to close it: the handover may not
  976	                    # take it and the receiver may never name it.
  977	                    refused.append((handle, kind))
  978	                    continue
  979	                if entry is not None:
  980	                    entry['state'] = self.TRANSFER_PENDING
  981	                accepted.append((handle, kind))
  982	            try:
  983	                register(accepted)
  984	            except BaseException:
  985	                # The receiver did not register the handles: they stay
  986	                # with the façade and their state is restored.  A half
  987	                # registered handle is never closed by two owners.
  988	                for handle, _kind in accepted:
  989	                    entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
  990	                    if entry is not None:
  991	                        entry['state'] = self.API_OWNED
  992	                raise
  993	            for handle, kind in accepted:
  994	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
  995	                if entry is not None:
  996	                    entry['state'] = self.TRANSFERRED
  997	                self._forget(handle)
  998	                self._handovers.append({'kind': kind, 'owner': owner,
  999	                                        'handle_repr': repr(handle)})
 1000	        if refused:
 1001	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDOVER_CLAIMED',
 1002	                              ','.join(repr(handle) for handle, _kind in refused))
 1003	        return True
 1004	
 1005	    def _claim_entry(self, key):
 1006	        """Claim one retained entry for the recovery of the façade.
 1007	
 1008	        The claim is **one atomic step** under the lock of the table:
 1009	        an entry that another recovery already claimed is never claimed
 1010	        a second time, and an entry that a handover took is never
 1011	        claimed at all.  The claim does not hold the lock of the table
 1012	        while it waits: the blocking primitive of the recovery runs
 1013	        outside of the lock and the table stays usable.
 1014	        """
 1015	        with self._table_lock:
 1016	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
 1017	            if entry is None:
 1018	                return None
 1019	            if entry.get('state') != self.API_OWNED:
 1020	                # The entry is claimed by a recovery that is running or
 1021	                # it is in the middle of a handover: it is not the owner
 1022	                # of this recovery.
 1023	                return None
 1024	            entry['state'] = self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED
 1025	            return dict(entry)
 1026	
 1027	    def _release_claim(self, key):
 1028	        """Give a claim back: a later recovery may claim the entry again."""
 1029	        with self._table_lock:
 1030	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
 1031	            if entry is not None and entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
 1032	                entry['state'] = self.API_OWNED
 1033	
 1034	    def _owns_claim(self, key):
 1035	        """Whether the claim of this recovery is still the owner of the entry."""
 1036	        with self._table_lock:
 1037	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
 1038	            if entry is None:
 1039	                return False
 1040	            return entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED
 1041	
 1042	    def _recovery_timeout_ms(self):
 1043	        """The milliseconds that are left until the ONE recovery deadline.
 1044	
 1045	        The deadline of the recovery is published **once**: a second
 1046	        recovery of the same round reuses the very same value and it
 1047	        never opens a new budget of its own.  It may only be **shortened**
 1048	        by a context that arrived later: a recovery that re-read the
 1049	        context of the round may not use the long lease of the round
 1050	        after the round published its cleanup deadline.
 1051	
 1052	        A wait of a recovery that arrives after the deadline never
 1053	        starts.
 1054	        """
 1055	        with self._table_lock:
 1056	            current = self._effective_deadline()
 1057	            if self._recovery_deadline is None:
 1058	                value = current
 1059	                if value is None:
 1060	                    value = self._clock() + (WAIT_MILLISECONDS / 1000.0)
 1061	                self._recovery_deadline = min(
 1062	                    value, self._clock() + (WAIT_MILLISECONDS / 1000.0))
 1063	            elif current is not None and current < self._recovery_deadline:
 1064	                self._recovery_deadline = current
 1065	            remaining = self._recovery_deadline - self._clock()
 1066	        if remaining <= 0:
 1067	            return 0
 1068	        return max(1, min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000)))
 1069	
 1070	    def _confirm_process_exit(self, handle, failures, cancellations,
 1071	                              timeout_ms=None):
 1072	        """Try to confirm the exit of one process, once.
 1073	
 1074	        The confirmation of the exit and the confirmation of the close
 1075	        of a handle are two different facts.  A wait that timed out is
 1076	        **not** an exit: the process may still be alive and it may even
 1077	        be a suspended process that never ran.  Its control handle is
 1078	        the only handle that may stop it.
 1079	
 1080	        The method returns ``True`` only when the exit was really
 1081	        confirmed.  A cancellation of the wait is named and it is handed
 1082	        to the caller, which raises it after every safe step was
 1083	        executed.
 1084	        """
 1085	        if timeout_ms is None:
 1086	            timeout_ms = self._wait_timeout_ms()
 1087	        if timeout_ms <= 0:
 1088	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:no_budget')
 1089	            return False
 1090	        try:
 1091	            result = self._api.kernel32.WaitForSingleObject(handle, timeout_ms)
 1092	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
 1093	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:CANCELLED')
 1094	            cancellations.append(error)
 1095	            return False
 1096	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 1097	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:' + type(error).__name__)
 1098	            return False
 1099	        if result == self.WAIT_OBJECT_0:
 1100	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:exited')
 1101	            return True
 1102	        if result == self.WAIT_TIMEOUT:
 1103	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:exit_not_confirmed')
 1104	            return False
 1105	        failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:wait=%d' % int(result))
 1106	        return False
 1107	
 1108	    def release_retained(self):
 1109	        """Try to release every handle that the façade still holds.
 1110	
 1111	        This is the recovery entry point of the façade.  It is explicit:
 1112	        a caller that wants the handles of a failed creation released
 1113	        asks for it, and the result is named.  A handle that could not
 1114	        be closed stays in the holding table.
 1115	
 1116	        Every handle is released on its own and every step of one handle
 1117	        is independent of the others: a cancellation in the middle of the
 1118	        recovery never skips the close of the next handle.  The
 1119	        cancellation is raised at the end of the recovery, after every
 1120	        safe step was executed, and it never replaces an ordinary
 1121	        failure of a handle.
 1122	
 1123	        The **control handle of a process** is released only when its
 1124	        exit was really confirmed.  A process whose exit was not
 1125	        confirmed may still be alive and its control handle is the only
 1126	        handle that may stop it.  The handle therefore stays in the
 1127	        holding table with its owner and the recovery of the façade can
 1128	        reach it again: a later recovery that confirms the exit closes
 1129	        it and forgets it.  A close that succeeds is never a proof that
 1130	        the process ended.
 1131	
 1132	        The recovery and the handover are **one ownership decision**.
 1133	        Every entry is **claimed** under the lock of the table before
 1134	        the recovery touches it, and a claim that is alive keeps every
 1135	        other recovery and every handover away from the entry.  The
 1136	        blocking primitive of the recovery runs **outside** of the lock
 1137	        of the table: the table is never held while the recovery waits.
 1138	        The claim is verified again before the close: an entry that
 1139	        changed its owner in the meantime is never closed.
 1140	        """
 1141	        report = []
 1142	        cancellations = []
 1143	        for key in list(self._retained_order):
 1144	            claimed = self._claim_entry(key)
 1145	            if claimed is None:
 1146	                # The entry is not owned by this recovery: it is claimed
 1147	                # by a recovery that is running or it was handed over.
 1148	                # It is never closed here.
 1149	                entry = self._retained.get(key)
 1150	                if entry is None:
 1151	                    continue
 1152	                report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
 1153	                               'released': False,
 1154	                               'failures': ['entry_not_claimed:' + str(
 1155	                                   entry.get('state'))]})
 1156	                continue
 1157	            entry = claimed
 1158	            handle = entry['handle']
 1159	            failures = []
 1160	            try:
 1161	                if entry['kind'] == 'process':
 1162	                    try:
 1163	                        if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateProcess(handle, 1):
 1164	                            failures.append('TerminateProcess:last_error=%d'
 1165	                                            % self._last_error())
 1166	                    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
 1167	                        failures.append('TerminateProcess:CANCELLED')
 1168	                        cancellations.append(error)
 1169	                    except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 1170	                        failures.append('TerminateProcess:' + type(error).__name__)
 1171	                    confirmed = self._confirm_process_exit(
 1172	                        handle, failures, cancellations,
 1173	                        timeout_ms=self._recovery_timeout_ms())
 1174	                    if not confirmed:
 1175	                        # The process may still be alive: the control
 1176	                        # handle stays with the façade and it is never
 1177	                        # closed under a process that nobody may stop
 1178	                        # any more.
 1179	                        failures.append('process_control_handle_retained:'
 1180	                                        'exit_not_confirmed')
 1181	                        self._note(handle, '|'.join(failures))
 1182	                        report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
 1183	                                       'released': False,
 1184	                                       'failures': list(failures)})
 1185	                        continue
 1186	                # The claim is verified again before the close: an entry
 1187	                # that changed its owner while the recovery waited is
 1188	                # never closed by this recovery.
 1189	                if not self._owns_claim(key):
 1190	                    failures.append('entry_claim_lost:before_close')
 1191	                    report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
 1192	                                   'released': False,
 1193	                                   'failures': list(failures)})
 1194	                    continue
 1195	                if self._close_quietly(handle, failures, cancellations):
 1196	                    self._forget(handle)
 1197	                else:
 1198	                    self._note(handle, '|'.join(failures))
 1199	                report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
 1200	                               'released': not any(failure.startswith('CloseHandle')
 1201	                                                   for failure in failures),
 1202	                               'failures': list(failures)})
 1203	            finally:
 1204	                # A claim that did not end with a confirmed close is
 1205	                # given back: a later recovery of the round may claim
 1206	                # the entry again and it may confirm the exit.
 1207	                self._release_claim(key)
 1208	        if cancellations:
 1209	            # The cancellation is raised after every safe step of the
 1210	            # recovery was executed.  It is never swallowed and it never
 1211	            # replaces an ordinary failure that is named in the report.
 1212	            raise cancellations[0]
 1213	        return report
 1214	
 1215	    # -- the job object ---------------------------------------------------
 1216	    def create_job(self):
 1217	        handle = self._api.kernel32.CreateJobObjectW(None, None)
 1218	        if not handle:
 1219	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_CREATE_FAILED',
 1220	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
 1221	        return handle
 1222	
 1223	    def set_job_kill_on_close(self, job):
 1224	        """Set the kill-on-close limit of the job and nothing else.
 1225	
 1226	        ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK`` is deliberately *not* set: a
 1227	        child of the round may not break away from the job.
 1228	        """
 1229	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
 1230	        limit = self._api.JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION()
 1231	        limit.BasicLimitInformation.LimitFlags = self.JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE
 1232	        if not self._api.kernel32.SetInformationJobObject(
 1233	                job, self.JOB_OBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
 1234	                ctypes.byref(limit), ctypes.sizeof(limit)):
 1235	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_LIMIT_FAILED',
 1236	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
 1237	        return True
 1238	
 1239	    def terminate_job(self, job, exit_code):
 1240	        if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateJobObject(job, exit_code):
 1241	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_TERMINATE_FAILED',
 1242	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
 1243	        return True
 1244	
 1245	    def job_process_ids(self, job):
 1246	        """The bounded list of the process ids of the job.
 1247	
 1248	        The query is bounded by the descendant cap of this package.  A
 1249	        job that holds more processes than the cap is *not* reported as an
 1250	        empty job: the answer is unknown, and an unknown answer never
 1251	        becomes a zero.
 1252	
 1253	        The structure of Windows holds two counts and an **inline** array
 1254	        of ``ULONG_PTR``.  The array is read from the buffer at the
 1255	        offset of the array and never through a pointer field.
 1256	
 1257	        The answer is only accepted when **every** fact of it agrees:
 1258	
 1259	        * the returned length is at least the size of the header, so that
 1260	          the two counts were really written and a legal empty job still
 1261	          carries its complete header;
 1262	        * the returned length is at most the size of the buffer, so that
 1263	          a length that is larger than the buffer was never accepted;
 1264	        * the returned length is at least the length that the count of
 1265	          the list needs, so that the list was not truncated;
 1266	        * the two counts are equal, so that the number of the assigned
 1267	          processes and the number of the listed processes agree;
 1268	        * the count of the list is within the cap of this package.
 1269	
 1270	        Every other result is **unknown**: an exception, a returned
 1271	        length of zero, a reversed pair of counts, a count over the cap
 1272	        and a truncated list.  An unknown answer is never an empty job
 1273	        and never a success.
 1274	        """
 1275	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
 1276	        capacity = MAX_DESCENDANTS + 1
 1277	        pointer_size = ctypes.sizeof(ctypes.c_size_t)
 1278	        header_size = 2 * ctypes.sizeof(self._api.types['DWORD'])
 1279	        size = header_size + capacity * pointer_size
 1280	        buffer = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size)
 1281	        returned = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
 1282	        if not self._api.kernel32.QueryInformationJobObject(
 1283	                job, self.JOB_OBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST, buffer, size,
 1284	                ctypes.byref(returned)):
 1285	            error = self._last_error()
 1286	            if error == self.ERROR_MORE_DATA:
 1287	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT', 'last_error=%d' % error)
 1288	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_UNKNOWN', 'last_error=%d' % error)
 1289	        header = ctypes.cast(buffer, ctypes.POINTER(
 1290	            self._api.JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST)).contents
 1291	        assigned = int(header.NumberOfAssignedProcesses)
 1292	        listed = int(header.NumberOfProcessIdsInList)
 1293	        reported = int(returned.value)
 1294	        # The returned length has to carry the whole header and it may
 1295	        # never be larger than the buffer that was handed to Windows.
 1296	        if reported < header_size or reported > size:
 1297	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
 1298	                              'returned=%d:header=%d:allocated=%d'
 1299	                              % (reported, header_size, size))
 1300	        if assigned > capacity or listed > capacity:
 1301	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT',
 1302	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
 1303	        if assigned != listed:
 1304	            # The two counts disagree: the answer is unknown and it is
 1305	            # never an empty job.
 1306	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
 1307	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
 1308	        if listed > MAX_DESCENDANTS:
 1309	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT',
 1310	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
 1311	        needed = header_size + listed * pointer_size
 1312	        if needed > reported:
 1313	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
 1314	                              'returned=%d:needed=%d' % (reported, needed))
 1315	        if listed == 0:
 1316	            # A legal empty job: the header was complete and the two
 1317	            # counts were zero.
 1318	            return []
 1319	        offset = header_size // pointer_size
 1320	        identifiers = ctypes.cast(buffer, ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_size_t))
 1321	        return [int(identifiers[offset + index]) for index in range(listed)]
 1322	
 1323	    # -- the processes -----------------------------------------------------
 1324	    def create_process_suspended(self, *, executable, arguments, working_directory,
 1325	                                  stdout_handle, stderr_handle, environment=None):
 1326	        """Create one suspended process and return its original handles.
 1327	
 1328	        The child inherits exactly three handles: the write end of its
 1329	        standard output, the write end of its standard error and the null
 1330	        device as its standard input.  The inheritance set is passed
 1331	        explicitly as the handle list of the process attributes, so that
 1332	        the control channel of the broker, the job handle and every other
 1333	        handle of the broker stay in the broker.
 1334	
 1335	        The size of the start up information is the size of the whole
 1336	        ``STARTUPINFOEXW``, because the extended flag is set and the
 1337	        attribute list follows the start up information inside it.
 1338	
 1339	        The creation has **independent** steps.  The deletion of the
 1340	        attribute list, the close of the null device, the birth query of
 1341	        the created process and the release of a process that could not
 1342	        be adopted do not depend on each other: a failure of one of them
 1343	        never skips the others.  The first error of the creation is the
 1344	        error that leaves the method and the later errors are named next
 1345	        to it; the first error is never replaced by a later one.
 1346	
 1347	        The original handles of a created process enter the holding table
 1348	        of this façade in the moment of the creation.  The null device
 1349	        of the child enters it in the moment of its creation as well: a
 1350	        null device that could not be closed is a resource of this round
 1351	        and it stays with its owner.  A handle that was handed over to
 1352	        the caller leaves the table; a handle that could not be closed
 1353	        stays in it with its owner and its failures, so that the recovery
 1354	        of the façade can always reach it.
 1355	        """
 1356	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
 1357	        kernel32 = self._api.kernel32
 1358	        failures = []
 1359	        command = ctypes.create_unicode_buffer(_windows_command_line(executable, arguments))
 1360	        environment_block = _environment_block(environment)
 1361	        attributes = self._security_attributes(True)
 1362	        null = kernel32.CreateFileW('NUL', self.GENERIC_READ,
 1363	                                    self.FILE_SHARE_READ | self.FILE_SHARE_WRITE,
 1364	                                    ctypes.byref(attributes), self.OPEN_EXISTING,
 1365	                                    self.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL, None)
 1366	        if not null or null == self.invalid_handle_value:
 1367	            error = self._last_error()
 1368	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_NULL_DEVICE_FAILED',
 1369	                              'last_error=%d' % error)
 1370	        # The moment of the creation of the null device.  The handle of
 1371	        # the null device enters the holding table of this façade right
 1372	        # here: a null device whose close fails later is a resource of
 1373	        # this round and it keeps its original owner and its recovery
 1374	        # entry point.
 1375	        self._retain(null, 'null_device')
 1376	        attribute_list = None
 1377	        information = None
 1378	        process = None
 1379	        thread = None
 1380	        pid = None
 1381	        primary = None
 1382	        cleanup_error = None
 1383	        cancellations = []
 1384	        try:
 1385	            try:
 1386	                inherited = (ctypes.c_void_p * 3)(stdout_handle, stderr_handle, null)
 1387	                size = ctypes.c_size_t(0)
 1388	                kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList(None, 1, 0, ctypes.byref(size))
 1389	                attribute_list = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size.value)
 1390	                if not kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList(
 1391	                        attribute_list, 1, 0, ctypes.byref(size)):
 1392	                    error = self._last_error()
 1393	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_FAILED',
 1394	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
 1395	                if not kernel32.UpdateProcThreadAttribute(
 1396	                        attribute_list, 0, self.PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST,
 1397	                        inherited, ctypes.sizeof(inherited), None, None):
 1398	                    error = self._last_error()
 1399	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_FAILED',
 1400	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
 1401	                startup = self._api.STARTUPINFOEXW()
 1402	                # The extended start up information is passed, so the
 1403	                # size is the size of the whole structure and not the
 1404	                # size of the plain start up information.
 1405	                startup.StartupInfo.cb = ctypes.sizeof(self._api.STARTUPINFOEXW)
 1406	                startup.StartupInfo.dwFlags = self.STARTF_USESTDHANDLES
 1407	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdInput = null
 1408	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdOutput = stdout_handle
 1409	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdError = stderr_handle
 1410	                startup.lpAttributeList = ctypes.cast(attribute_list, ctypes.c_void_p)
 1411	                information = self._api.PROCESS_INFORMATION()
 1412	                flags = (self.CREATE_SUSPENDED | self.EXTENDED_STARTUPINFO_PRESENT
 1413	                         | self.CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT)
 1414	                environment_pointer = (ctypes.cast(environment_block, ctypes.c_void_p)
 1415	                                       if environment_block is not None else None)
 1416	                created = kernel32.CreateProcessW(
 1417	                    str(executable), command, None, None, True, flags,
 1418	                    environment_pointer, str(working_directory),
 1419	                    ctypes.byref(startup.StartupInfo), ctypes.byref(information))
 1420	                if not created:
 1421	                    error = self._last_error()
 1422	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_PROCESS_CREATE_FAILED',
 1423	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
 1424	                # The moment of the creation.  The original handles of
 1425	                # the process enter the holding table of this façade
 1426	                # before anything else may fail: they are never only an
 1427	                # integer in a local list or in the detail of an error.
 1428	                process = information.hProcess
 1429	                thread = information.hThread
 1430	                pid = int(information.dwProcessId)
 1431	                self._retain(process, 'process', pid)
 1432	                self._retain(thread, 'thread', pid)
 1433	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 1434	                primary = error
 1435	            finally:
 1436	                # The cleanups of the creation are independent of each
 1437	                # other and independent of the failure that is on the way
 1438	                # out.  A cleanup that fails never skips the other one and
 1439	                # a cancellation in the middle of a cleanup never skips
 1440	                # the cleanup that follows it: the cancellation is named
 1441	                # and it is raised after every safe step was executed.
 1442	                attribute_failures = []
 1443	                if attribute_list is not None:
 1444	                    try:
 1445	                        kernel32.DeleteProcThreadAttributeList(attribute_list)
 1446	                    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
 1447	                        attribute_failures.append('DeleteProcThreadAttributeList:CANCELLED')
 1448	                        cancellations.append(error)
 1449	                    except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 1450	                        attribute_failures.append('DeleteProcThreadAttributeList:'
 1451	                                                  + type(error).__name__)
 1452	                null_failures = []
 1453	                if self._close_quietly(null, null_failures, cancellations):
 1454	                    # The null device was closed with a confirmed close:
 1455	                    # it may be forgotten.
 1456	                    self._forget(null)
 1457	                failures.extend(attribute_failures)
 1458	                failures.extend(null_failures)
 1459	                if attribute_failures:
 1460	                    cleanup_error = BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_CLEANUP_FAILED',
 1461	                                                '|'.join(attribute_failures))
 1462	                elif null_failures:
 1463	                    cleanup_error = BrokerError('BROKER_NULL_DEVICE_CLOSE_FAILED',
 1464	                                                '|'.join(null_failures))
 1465	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 1466	            if primary is None:
 1467	                primary = error
 1468	        if cancellations and primary is None:
 1469	            # The cancellation of a cleanup is the primary error of the
 1470	            # creation when nothing else failed.  The cleanups that were
 1471	            # safe were executed before it was raised and the ordinary
 1472	            # failures that were named stay named next to it.
 1473	            primary = cancellations[0]
 1474	            failures.append('creation_cancelled:' + type(primary).__name__)
 1475	        creation = None
 1476	        if process is not None:
 1477	            # The birth query is the identity of the created process.  It
 1478	            # is independent of the cleanups above: it always runs when a
 1479	            # process was created.
 1480	            try:
 1481	                creation = self._process_creation_time(process)
 1482	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 1483	                if primary is None:
 1484	                    primary = error
 1485	        if primary is None and cleanup_error is None:
 1486	            # The creation was handed over: the original handles are the
 1487	            # property of the caller now and they leave the holding table.
 1488	            self._forget(process)
 1489	            self._forget(thread)
 1490	            return CreatedProcess(process, thread, pid, creation, failures)
 1491	        # The creation failed.  A process that was created is released
 1492	        # through its own original handle and every failure is named.
 1493	        release_failures = []
 1494	        if process is not None:
 1495	            self._release_created(process, thread, release_failures, cancellations)
 1496	        failures.extend(release_failures)
 1497	        if cancellations and primary is None:
 1498	            # The cancellation of the release is the primary error of the
 1499	            # creation when nothing else failed.  Every safe step was
 1500	            # executed before it is raised.
 1501	            primary = cancellations[0]
 1502	            failures.append('creation_cancelled:' + type(primary).__name__)
 1503	        error = primary if primary is not None else cleanup_error
 1504	        detail = getattr(error, 'detail', None)
 1505	        if failures:
 1506	            error.detail = ((detail or '') + '|cleanup=' + ','.join(failures))
 1507	        if cancellations and error is not cancellations[0]:
 1508	            # The primary error and the cancellation coexist: the
 1509	            # cancellation object stays reachable next to the primary
 1510	            # error and it is never replaced by a copy of a message.
 1511	            try:
 1512	                error.lvw_cancelled = cancellations[0]
 1513	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
