 3013	        The pass never closes a handle whose owner is still using it:
 3014	        the guard is the fact of the owner and not the name of an
 3015	        owner.
 3016	        """
 3017	        retained = self._pending.close_all(self.failures,
 3018	                                           on_closed=self._note_closed_handle,
 3019	                                           protect=self._pending_handle_in_use)
 3020	        if retained:
 3021	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'pending_resources',
 3022	                                  'count': len(retained),
 3023	                                  'code': 'BROKER_PENDING_RESOURCES_RETAINED'})
 3024	
 3025	    def _note_closed_handle(self, item):
 3026	        """Learn that one handle was really closed.
 3027	
 3028	        The bookkeeping of the core has to agree with the fact of the
 3029	        close: a handle that is closed may never be named by the job of
 3030	        the round, by the records of the round or by the retained
 3031	        resources of the round.  Only the handle that was really closed
 3032	        is forgotten: a handle that could not be closed keeps its owner
 3033	        and it stays named.
 3034	
 3035	        The **owner object** of the handle is notified as well.  A
 3036	        reader whose read end was closed by the pending pass learns that
 3037	        the handle is gone: a later stop never closes the same original
 3038	        handle a second time and it never closes a handle that the
 3039	        operating system may have reused in the meantime.
 3040	        """
 3041	        handle = item['handle']
 3042	        kind = item.get('kind')
 3043	        if self.job is handle:
 3044	            self.job = None
 3045	            self.job_closed = True
 3046	        for record in self.records:
 3047	            if record.get('process_handle') is handle:
 3048	                record['process_handle_closed'] = True
 3049	                record['process_handle'] = None
 3050	            if record.get('thread_handle') is handle:
 3051	                record['thread_handle_closed'] = True
 3052	                record['thread_handle'] = None
 3053	        owner = item.get('owner_ref')
 3054	        if owner is not None and getattr(owner, 'handle', None) is handle:
 3055	            # The owner of the handle learns that it is closed.  The
 3056	            # handle of the owner is the **original** handle and it is
 3057	            # never closed twice.
 3058	            try:
 3059	                owner.handle = None
 3060	                owner.closed = True
 3061	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 3062	                pass
 3063	        self._release_resource(kind, handle)
 3064	
 3065	    def _terminate_the_job(self):
 3066	        """Terminate the job of the round.
 3067	
 3068	        The termination happens once.  A repeated stop does not terminate
 3069	        the job of the round a second time.
 3070	        """
 3071	        if self.job is None or self.job_terminated:
 3072	            return
 3073	        try:
 3074	            self.win32.terminate_job(self.job, 1)
 3075	            self.job_terminated = True
 3076	        except BrokerError as error:
 3077	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'terminate_job', 'code': error.code})
 3078	
 3079	    def _wait_the_processes(self):
 3080	        """Wait for the original processes of the round.
 3081	
 3082	        Every process is independent of the others: a process that could
 3083	        not be waited for does not skip the wait of the next one, and an
 3084	        ordinary failure of the wait of one process does not interrupt
 3085	        the group.  The wait is bounded by the ONE cleanup deadline of
 3086	        the round and the remaining budget is recomputed before every
 3087	        single wait: an expired deadline never starts a new wait.
 3088	        """
 3089	        for record in self.records:
 3090	            try:
 3091	                self._wait_one_process(record)
 3092	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
 3093	                # A cancellation never skips the processes that follow it:
 3094	                # it is named and it is raised after every process was
 3095	                # coordinated.
 3096	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
 3097	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__,
 3098	                                      'cancellation': True})
 3099	                self._processes_cancelled.append(error)
 3100	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 3101	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
 3102	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__})
 3103	        if self._processes_cancelled:
 3104	            raise self._processes_cancelled[0]
 3105	
 3106	    def _wait_one_process(self, record):
 3107	        """Wait for one original process of the round, on its own."""
 3108	        handle = record.get('process_handle')
 3109	        if handle is None or record['exited']:
 3110	            return
 3111	        if not record['resumed']:
 3112	            # The process never ran: it is stopped through its own
 3113	            # suspended handle.
 3114	            self._terminate_owned(handle, record, 'SUSPENDED_AT_STOP')
 3115	            return
 3116	        timeout = self._wait_timeout_ms()
 3117	        if timeout <= 0:
 3118	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
 3119	                                  'code': 'BROKER_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'})
 3120	            return
 3121	        try:
 3122	            record['exited'] = bool(self.win32.wait_process(handle, timeout))
 3123	        except BrokerError as error:
 3124	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
 3125	                                  'code': error.code})
 3126	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
 3127	            # A cancellation is never turned into an ordinary evidence:
 3128	            # it leaves the wait of this process and it is raised after
 3129	            # every other process was coordinated.
 3130	            raise
 3131	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 3132	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
 3133	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
 3134	            return
 3135	        if record['exited']:
 3136	            try:
 3137	                record['exit_code'] = self.win32.exit_code(handle)
 3138	            except BrokerError as error:
 3139	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'exit_code', 'role': record['role'],
 3140	                                      'code': error.code})
 3141	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
 3142	                # The cancellation of the exit code is a cancellation of
 3143	                # the round and it is never swallowed by the evidence.
 3144	                raise
 3145	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 3146	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'exit_code', 'role': record['role'],
 3147	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__})
 3148	
 3149	    def _wait_timeout_ms(self):
 3150	        """The milliseconds that are left until the cleanup deadline."""
 3151	        remaining = self.remaining_cleanup_seconds()
 3152	        if remaining <= 0:
 3153	            return 0
 3154	        return max(1, min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000)))
 3155	
 3156	    def _release_the_records(self):
 3157	        """Close the original handles of the processes of the round.
 3158	
 3159	        A handle that was closed with a confirmed close is forgotten and
 3160	        it is never closed a second time.  A handle that could not be
 3161	        closed keeps its original reference and its owner.
 3162	
 3163	        The **control handle of a process** is closed **only** when the
 3164	        exit of that process was really confirmed.  A process whose exit
 3165	        was not confirmed - a suspended child that was never assigned to
 3166	        the job of the round, a process whose wait timed out or whose
 3167	        wait failed - may still be alive, and its control handle is the
 3168	        only handle that may stop it.  The handle therefore stays with
 3169	        the record of the process and with the pending table of the
 3170	        round, and the same owner may confirm the exit and close it
 3171	        later, inside the same cleanup deadline of the round.
 3172	
 3173	        The thread handle and the pipe handles are independent of the
 3174	        exit of the process: they are closed on their own and they never
 3175	        wait for the process.
 3176	
 3177	        Every handle of every record is closed on its own: a failure of
 3178	        one of them never skips the handles that follow it, and a
 3179	        cancellation is raised after every handle was coordinated.
 3180	        """
 3181	        cancellations = []
 3182	        for record in self.records:
 3183	            for key, kind in (('thread_handle', 'thread'),
 3184	                              ('process_handle', 'process')):
 3185	                handle = record.get(key)
 3186	                if handle is None:
 3187	                    continue
 3188	                if kind == 'process' and not self._process_closeable(record):
 3189	                    # The exit of the process was not confirmed: the
 3190	                    # control handle stays with its owner and the round
 3191	                    # keeps the fact.  The handle is never closed under
 3192	                    # a process that nobody may stop any more.
 3193	                    record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = False
 3194	                    self._retain_resource(
 3195	                        kind, handle, 'BROKER_PROCESS_EXIT_NOT_CONFIRMED')
 3196	                    self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle',
 3197	                                          'kind': kind,
 3198	                                          'role': record.get('role'),
 3199	                                          'code': 'BROKER_PROCESS_EXIT_NOT_CONFIRMED'})
 3200	                    continue
 3201	                try:
 3202	                    closed = self._close_handle(kind, handle)
 3203	                except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
 3204	                    # A cancellation never skips the handles that follow
 3205	                    # it: it is named and it is raised after every
 3206	                    # handle was coordinated.
 3207	                    record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = False
 3208	                    self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle', 'kind': kind,
 3209	                                          'role': record.get('role'),
 3210	                                          'error_type': type(error).__name__,
 3211	                                          'cancellation': True})
 3212	                    self._retain_resource(kind, handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
 3213	                    cancellations.append(error)
 3214	                    continue
 3215	                record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = closed
 3216	                if closed:
 3217	                    record[key] = None
 3218	                else:
 3219	                    self._retain_resource(kind, handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
 3220	        if cancellations:
 3221	            raise cancellations[0]
 3222	
 3223	    def _release_the_job(self):
 3224	        """Close the original handle of the job of the round.
 3225	
 3226	        A job handle that could not be closed is **not** forgotten: the
 3227	        core keeps the original reference and the round stays unknown.
 3228	        """
 3229	        if self.job is None:
 3230	            return
 3231	        handle = self.job
 3232	        closed = self._close_handle('job', handle)
 3233	        self.job_closed = closed
 3234	        if closed:
 3235	            self.job = None
 3236	            self._release_resource('job', handle)
 3237	        else:
 3238	            self._retain_resource('job', handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
 3239	
 3240	    def _is_released(self):
 3241	        """Whether the round is a proven release.
 3242	
 3243	        A release needs every fact and not only the absence of a failure:
 3244	        every owned process has to have exited, every handle has to have
 3245	        been closed, every reader has to have been joined without a read
 3246	        error and the job has to have been queried and found empty.
 3247	        """
 3248	        if self.failures:
 3249	            return False
 3250	        if not all(record['exited'] for record in self.records if record['created']):
 3251	            return False
 3252	        if not all(record['process_handle_closed'] and record['thread_handle_closed']
 3253	                   for record in self.records):
 3254	            return False
 3255	        if not self.job_closed:
 3256	            return False
 3257	        if not (self.job_active_known and self.job_active == 0):
 3258	            return False
 3259	        if not self.reader_states:
 3260	            return False
 3261	        for state in self.reader_states:
 3262	            if not state['finished'] or state['alive'] or state['error']:
 3263	                return False
 3264	        if self._pending.pending():
 3265	            return False
 3266	        return True
 3267	
 3268	    def _query_job(self):
 3269	        """Ask the job how many processes it still holds.
 3270	
 3271	        The answer is bounded.  An unknown answer and an answer that is
 3272	        over the cap are both *unknown*: neither of them is a zero.
 3273	
 3274	        The query only runs while the job handle is still open.  A
 3275	        handle that was already closed keeps the answer that it produced:
 3276	        a second stop never destroys the proof of the first one.
 3277	        """
 3278	        if self.job is None:
 3279	            return
 3280	        try:
 3281	            identifiers = self.win32.job_process_ids(self.job)
 3282	        except BrokerError as error:
 3283	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'query_job', 'code': error.code})
 3284	            self.job_active_known = False
 3285	            self.job_active = None
 3286	            return
 3287	        if len(identifiers) > MAX_DESCENDANTS:
 3288	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'query_job', 'code': 'BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT'})
 3289	            self.job_active_known = False
 3290	            self.job_active = None
 3291	            return
 3292	        self.job_active_known = True
 3293	        self.job_active = len(identifiers)
 3294	        self.job_process_ids = list(identifiers)
 3295	
 3296	    def _collect_stream_counts(self):
 3297	        total = 0
 3298	        for record in self.records:
 3299	            for stream, key in (('stdout', 'stdout_bytes'), ('stderr', 'stderr_bytes')):
 3300	                for reader in self.readers:
 3301	                    if reader.name.endswith(record['role'] + '-' + stream):
 3302	                        record[key] = reader.observed
 3303	                        record[stream + '_overflow'] = reader.overflow
 3304	                        if reader.overflow and not self._stream_failure_reported:
 3305	                            self._stream_failure_reported = True
 3306	                            self.failures.append({'stage': 'stream_limit',
 3307	                                                  'role': record['role'],
 3308	                                                  'stream': stream,
 3309	                                                  'code': 'BROKER_STREAM_LIMIT_EXCEEDED'})
 3310	        for reader in self.readers:
 3311	            total += reader.kept
 3312	        self.new_text_bytes = total
 3313	        if total > MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES and not self._text_failure_reported:
 3314	            self._text_failure_reported = True
 3315	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'new_text_limit',
 3316	                                  'code': 'BROKER_NEW_TEXT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED'})
 3317	
 3318	    def overflowed(self):
 3319	        """Whether a bound of the text of the children was exceeded."""
 3320	        self._collect_stream_counts()
 3321	        if self.new_text_bytes > MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES:
 3322	            return True
 3323	        return any(record['stdout_overflow'] or record['stderr_overflow']
 3324	                   for record in self.records)
 3325	
 3326	    def _abort_round(self, reason):
 3327	        """Fail the round closed.
 3328	
 3329	        The stop of the round is bounded and it only touches the handles
 3330	        and the job of this round.  The state of the round is unknown
 3331	        afterwards, whatever the stop itself proved.  The abort is
 3332	        idempotent: a failure that already aborted the round does not
 3333	        abort it a second time and it does not report it twice.
 3334	        """
 3335	        if not self._abort_reported:
 3336	            self._abort_reported = True
 3337	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'round', 'code': reason})
 3338	        try:
 3339	            self.stop(reason, force_unknown=True)
 3340	        except BrokerError as error:
 3341	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'abort', 'code': error.code})
 3342	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 3343	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'abort', 'error_type': type(error).__name__})
 3344	        self.state = 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
 3345	
 3346	    # -- the evidence ------------------------------------------------------
 3347	    def summary(self):
 3348	        """The bounded summary of the round that fits into one frame."""
 3349	        return {'state': self.state,
 3350	                'roles': [{'role': record['role'], 'pid': record['pid'],
 3351	                           'created': record['created'], 'assigned': record['assigned'],
 3352	                           'resumed': record['resumed'], 'exited': record['exited']}
 3353	                          for record in self.records],
 3354	                'job_active_known': self.job_active_known,
 3355	                'job_active': self.job_active,
 3356	                'failures': len(self.failures)}
 3357	
 3358	    def evidence(self):
 3359	        return {
 3360	            'kind': 'lvw.s3-native-broker-evidence/1',
 3361	            'state': self.state,
 3362	            'run_id': self.plan.run_id,
 3363	            'candidate_sha': self.plan.candidate_sha,
 3364	            'authorization_sha256': self.plan.authorization_sha256,
 3365	            'grant_authority': 'NOT_VERIFIED',
 3366	            'stop_reason': self.stop_reason,
 3367	            'broker': self.broker_identity,
 3368	            'job': {'created': self.job is not None or self.job_closed,
 3369	                    'kill_on_job_close': self.job_kill_on_close,
 3370	                    'breakaway_allowed': False,
 3371	                    'terminated': self.job_terminated,
 3372	                    'active_process_count': self.job_active,
 3373	                    'active_process_count_known': self.job_active_known,
 3374	                    'process_ids': list(self.job_process_ids),
 3375	                    'handle_closed': self.job_closed},
 3376	            'roles': [{key: value for key, value in record.items()
 3377	                       if key not in ('process_handle', 'thread_handle')}
 3378	                      for record in self.records],
 3379	            'new_text_bytes': self.new_text_bytes,
 3380	            'readers': list(self.reader_states),
 3381	            'pending_resources': self._pending.pending(),
 3382	            # The resources that this round could not release and the
 3383	            # original handles that the Windows façade still holds.  They
 3384	            # are named with their original references: a resource that
 3385	            # was not released is never replaced by a copy of a message.
 3386	            'retained_resources': [{'kind': item['kind'], 'owner': item['owner'],
 3387	                                    'code': item['code'],
 3388	                                    'handle_repr': repr(item['handle'])}
 3389	                                   for item in self._retained_resources],
 3390	            'api_retained': self._api_retained(),
 3391	            'cleanup_deadline': self._cleanup_deadline_value,
 3392	            'failures': list(self.failures),
 3393	            # The claims that this package does not make.
 3394	            'native_execution_proven': False,
 3395	            'native_cleanup_proven': False,
 3396	            'gpu_released_proven': False,
 3397	            'port_released_proven': False,
 3398	            'sampler_proven': False,
 3399	            'parent_crash_recovery_proven': False,
 3400	            'windows_stdin_eof_proven': False,
 3401	            'admission_produced': False,
 3402	            'stage_pass_claim': False,
 3403	            'product_gate0': False,
 3404	        }
 3405	
 3406	
 3407	# -- the bounded private pipe ----------------------------------------------
 3408	
 3409	def _require_deadline(deadline, code='BROKER_DEADLINE_INVALID'):
 3410	    """Check one absolute deadline.
 3411	
 3412	    A deadline is a finite number of seconds of the monotonic clock of
 3413	    the host.  A boolean, a string, an infinite value and a value that
 3414	    is not a number at all are refused: a deadline that was understood
 3415	    as something else would silently unbound the round.
 3416	    """
 3417	    if deadline is None:
 3418	        return None
 3419	    if isinstance(deadline, bool) or not isinstance(deadline, (int, float)):
 3420	        raise BrokerError(code)
 3421	    value = float(deadline)
 3422	    if not math.isfinite(value):
 3423	        raise BrokerError(code)
 3424	    return value
 3425	
 3426	
 3427	class _PosixSyscalls:
 3428	    """The POSIX system calls of the private pipe.
 3429	
 3430	    The table is a narrow and explicit interface.  It is the only place
 3431	    where the private channel touches the operating system, and it can
 3432	    be replaced by a synthetic table in the offline validation: the
 3433	    production logic of the channel is then driven by a table that
 3434	    records the calls instead of by the kernel.
 3435	    """
 3436	
 3437	    def __init__(self):
 3438	        self._pollers = {}
 3439	
 3440	    def fileno(self, file_object):
 3441	        return file_object.fileno()
 3442	
 3443	    def set_blocking(self, descriptor, blocking):
 3444	        os.set_blocking(descriptor, blocking)
 3445	
 3446	    def _poller(self, descriptor):
 3447	        poller = self._pollers.get(descriptor)
 3448	        if poller is None:
 3449	            poller = select.poll()
 3450	            poller.register(descriptor,
 3451	                            select.POLLIN | select.POLLOUT
 3452	                            | select.POLLERR | select.POLLHUP)
 3453	            self._pollers[descriptor] = poller
 3454	        return poller
 3455	
 3456	    def poll(self, descriptor, mask, timeout):
 3457	        """Whether the descriptor is ready for the requested operation."""
 3458	        ready = self._poller(descriptor).poll(max(0.0, timeout) * 1000)
 3459	        for number, event in ready:
 3460	            if event & select.POLLNVAL:
 3461	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_DESCRIPTOR_INVALID')
 3462	            if mask == 'read' and event & (select.POLLIN | select.POLLHUP
 3463	                                           | select.POLLERR):
 3464	                return True
 3465	            if mask == 'write' and event & (select.POLLOUT | select.POLLHUP
 3466	                                            | select.POLLERR):
 3467	                return True
 3468	        return False
 3469	
 3470	    def read(self, descriptor, size):
 3471	        return os.read(descriptor, size)
 3472	
 3473	    def write(self, descriptor, data):
 3474	        return os.write(descriptor, data)
 3475	
 3476	    def close(self, file_object):
 3477	        file_object.close()
 3478	
 3479	
 3480	class _PosixPipeEndpoint:
 3481	    """One end of a private pipe on a POSIX host.
 3482	
 3483	    The endpoint owns the original descriptor of the pipe and it closes
 3484	    it itself.  The descriptor is set to non blocking exactly once, by
 3485	    the owner that created it, and the read and the write poll it in
 3486	    slices of at most 50 milliseconds.  Every slice checks the cancel
 3487	    flag and the absolute deadline, so that a peer that never reads can
 3488	    never hold the supervisor of the round.
 3489	
 3490	    The endpoint never changes the blocking mode of a descriptor that it
 3491	    does not own and it never closes a descriptor that it does not own.
 3492	    It never relies on the lock of a buffered object either: the close
 3493	    of a buffered object may wait for a reader that holds its lock, and
 3494	    the close of this endpoint is the close of the owner of the
 3495	    descriptor.
 3496	    """
 3497	
 3498	    def __init__(self, file_object, *, name, syscalls=None, clock=None):
 3499	        self._file = file_object
 3500	        self._name = name
 3501	        self._syscalls = syscalls or _PosixSyscalls()
 3502	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
 3503	        self._descriptor = self._syscalls.fileno(file_object)
 3504	        self._non_blocking = False
 3505	        self._closed = False
 3506	        # The absolute deadline that is bound to this endpoint.  It may
 3507	        # only shorten the deadline that is already bound and it is read
 3508	        # again on every slice: a deadline that was bound after a read
 3509	        # started still tightens that read.
 3510	        self._deadline = None
 3511	        self.facts = {'name': name, 'owner': 'POSIX_ENDPOINT',
 3512	                      'descriptor': self._descriptor}
 3513	
 3514	    def bind_deadline(self, deadline):
 3515	        """Bind an absolute deadline to this endpoint.
 3516	
 3517	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
 3518	        bound.  It is a property of the endpoint and not a local
 3519	        argument of one call: a call that passed ``None`` before the
 3520	        binding still observes the bound deadline.
 3521	        """
 3522	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
 3523	        if value is None:
 3524	            return self._deadline
 3525	        if self._deadline is not None and value > self._deadline:
 3526	            return self._deadline
 3527	        self._deadline = value
 3528	        return value
 3529	
 3530	    def _effective_deadline(self, deadline=None):
 3531	        """The deadline that this call really uses."""
 3532	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
 3533	        if self._deadline is None:
 3534	            return value
 3535	        if value is None:
 3536	            return self._deadline
 3537	        return min(value, self._deadline)
 3538	
 3539	    def prepare(self):
 3540	        """Set the descriptor to non blocking, once, by its owner."""
 3541	        if self._non_blocking or self._closed:
 3542	            return
 3543	        self._syscalls.set_blocking(self._descriptor, False)
 3544	        self._non_blocking = True
 3545	        self.facts['non_blocking'] = True
 3546	
 3547	    def _check(self, deadline, cancel):
 3548	        if cancel is not None and cancel.is_set():
 3549	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name)
 3550	        if deadline is not None and self._clock() >= deadline:
 3551	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self._name)
 3552	
 3553	    def read(self, deadline=None, cancel=None):
 3554	        """Read one block of the pipe, bounded by the deadline."""
 3555	        self.prepare()
 3556	        while True:
 3557	            self._check(self._effective_deadline(deadline), cancel)
 3558	            if not self._syscalls.poll(self._descriptor, 'read',
 3559	                                       POLL_SLICE_SECONDS):
 3560	                continue
 3561	            try:
 3562	                data = self._syscalls.read(self._descriptor, PIPE_READ_BYTES)
 3563	            except BlockingIOError:
 3564	                # The pipe was not ready after all: the slice is repeated.
 3565	                continue
 3566	            except OSError as error:
 3567	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_READ_FAILED',
 3568	                                  '%s:%s' % (self._name, type(error).__name__))
 3569	            if not data:
 3570	                return b''
 3571	            return data
 3572	
 3573	    def write(self, data, deadline=None, cancel=None):
 3574	        """Write the whole block, bounded by the deadline."""
 3575	        self.prepare()
 3576	        view = memoryview(bytes(data))
 3577	        total = 0
 3578	        while total < len(view):
 3579	            self._check(self._effective_deadline(deadline), cancel)
 3580	            if not self._syscalls.poll(self._descriptor, 'write',
 3581	                                       POLL_SLICE_SECONDS):
 3582	                continue
 3583	            try:
 3584	                count = self._syscalls.write(self._descriptor, view[total:])
 3585	            except BlockingIOError:
 3586	                # The pipe was full after all: the slice is repeated.
 3587	                continue
 3588	            except OSError as error:
 3589	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_WRITE_FAILED',
 3590	                                  '%s:%s' % (self._name, type(error).__name__))
 3591	            if count <= 0:
 3592	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_WRITE_FAILED', self._name)
 3593	            total += count
 3594	        return total
 3595	
 3596	    def close(self):
 3597	        """Close the original descriptor of this endpoint, exactly once.
 3598	
 3599	        The endpoint is closed **only** when the close really happened.
 3600	        A close that failed keeps the descriptor and its owner: the
 3601	        endpoint may try it again and it is never marked as closed
 3602	        irreversibly.
 3603	
 3604	        A close of an endpoint that is already closed keeps the fact
 3605	        that it is closed: the second close is idempotent and it never
 3606	        turns a proven close into a missing one.
 3607	        """
 3608	        if self._closed:
 3609	            self.facts['closed'] = True
 3610	            self.facts['already_closed'] = True
 3611	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
 3612	            self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
 3613	            return self.facts
 3614	        try:
 3615	            self._syscalls.close(self._file)
 3616	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 3617	            self.facts['closed'] = False
 3618	            self.facts['error_type'] = type(error).__name__
 3619	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
 3620	            self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
 3621	            return self.facts
 3622	        self._closed = True
 3623	        self.facts['closed'] = True
 3624	        # This host has no worker thread for an end of the pipe: the
 3625	        # blocking calls are made by the reader of the channel itself.
 3626	        # There is therefore no original worker handle that could be
 3627	        # outstanding, and the fact says so.
 3628	        self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
 3629	        self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
 3630	        return self.facts
 3631	
 3632	
 3633	class _WindowsPipeWorker(threading.Thread):
 3634	    """One worker of one end of a private pipe on a Windows host.
 3635	
 3636	    The worker owns one blocking operation at a time.  It takes the
 3637	    request from its queue, it performs the blocking call on the
 3638	    original handle of the pipe and it reports the real completion of
 3639	    the call.  The thread is created once for the endpoint and it is
 3640	    never created for a frame.
 3641	
 3642	    The handle of the thread is the handle of the thread itself and
 3643	    never a self reported identifier: ``CancelSynchronousIo`` needs the
 3644	    original handle of the thread that performs the operation.
 3645	
 3646	    The lifecycle of the worker is **explicit**.  The states are
 3647	    ``IDLE``, ``QUEUED``, ``IN_IO``, ``CANCELLING``, ``DONE`` and
 3648	    ``STOPPED``:
 3649	
 3650	    * ``IDLE``      the worker waits for a request;
 3651	    * ``QUEUED``    a request waits for the worker;
 3652	    * ``IN_IO``     the worker is inside the blocking call;
 3653	    * ``CANCELLING`` a cancellation was asked for;
 3654	    * ``DONE``      the request of the worker completed;
 3655	    * ``STOPPED``   the thread of the worker really ended.
 3656	
 3657	    ``DONE`` is not ``STOPPED``: the completion of a request says
 3658	    nothing about the thread of the worker, and the handle of the
 3659	    thread may only be closed after the thread really ended.
 3660	
 3661	    The worker is the **only** owner of the handle of its own thread:
 3662	    it duplicated that handle and it closes it when its thread really
 3663	    ended.  A worker that did not stop keeps its handle and its owner:
 3664	    the handle is never closed under a live worker and it is never left
 3665	    to the garbage collector.
 3666	    """
 3667	
 3668	    STATES = ('IDLE', 'QUEUED', 'IN_IO', 'CANCELLING', 'DONE', 'STOPPED')
 3669	
 3670	    def __init__(self, api, handle, *, kind, action, name, clock=None,
 3671	                 context=None):
 3672	        super().__init__(daemon=True, name=name)
 3673	        self.api = api
 3674	        self.handle = handle
 3675	        self.kind = kind
 3676	        self.action = action
 3677	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
 3678	        self._requests = queue.Queue(maxsize=1)
 3679	        # The event of the in-flight operation.  It is set while the
 3680	        # worker is inside the call and it is cleared again when the
 3681	        # call ended: an event that is never cleared would report a
 3682	        # permanent in-flight state.
 3683	        self.inside = threading.Event()
 3684	        self.done = threading.Event()
 3685	        self.stopped = threading.Event()
 3686	        self.ready = threading.Event()
 3687	        self.result = None
 3688	        self.error = None
 3689	        # The **original** error object of the last request.  The code
 3690	        # of the error is a name; the object is the failure.  It is kept
 3691	        # so that the endpoint may raise the very object that the
 3692	        # primitive raised instead of a copy of its name.
 3693	        self._error_object = None
 3694	        self.thread_handle = None
 3695	        self.thread_handle_released = False
 3696	        self.joined = False
 3697	        self.state = 'IDLE'
 3698	        # The number of the requests that were submitted and the number
 3699	        # of the requests that completed.  The two numbers are the real
 3700	        # completion of a request: the event alone could report the
 3701	        # completion of an older request.
 3702	        self._condition = threading.Condition()
 3703	        self._submitted = 0
 3704	        self._completed = 0
 3705	        # The generations of the requests that were handed over, in the
 3706	        # order in which they were handed over.  The worker takes the
 3707	        # request and its generation together: a worker that would read
 3708	        # the number of the submissions would name the generation of a
 3709	        # later request.
 3710	        self._generations = collections.deque()
 3711	        # The **outcome of every generation**.  The outcome carries the
 3712	        # result, the error, the **original error object**, the source
 3713	        # of the failure and the order of the completion.  A result
 3714	        # that was really published is never replaced by a later one and
 3715	        # a cancellation never rewrites the outcome of a generation.
 3716	        self._outcomes = {}
 3717	        # The explicit notification that a **failure** of a generation
 3718	        # was really published.  It is set under the lock of the worker
 3719	        # and it is set **after** the outcome was recorded: an owner
 3720	        # that waits for it knows that the failure of the round is
 3721	        # published and that a later cancellation may not replace it.
 3722	        self.outcome_published = threading.Event()
 3723	        # The order of the events of this worker.  Every completion and
 3724	        # every cancellation takes a number of it, under the lock of the
 3725	        # worker: the number tells whether a failure was published
 3726	        # **before** the cancellation of the round or after it.
 3727	        self._sequence = 0
 3728	        self._cancel_sequence = None
 3729	        # The ONE context of the round.  The worker reads the absolute
 3730	        # deadline and the published cleanup deadline of the round from
 3731	        # it and it never keeps a budget of its own.
 3732	        self._context = context
 3733	        # The flag of the cancellation.  It stops the worker from
 3734	        # accepting a new task before the cancellation is requested.
 3735	        self._cancelled = False
 3736	        # The name of the cancel flag is not ``_stop``: the base class of
 3737	        # the thread uses that name for its own method and a shadow of it
 3738	        # would break the join of the thread.
 3739	        self._stop_event = threading.Event()
 3740	        # The pause of the retry of a cancellation.  It is its **own**
 3741	        # event and it is never set: the retry of a cancellation may not
 3742	        # wait on the stop event of the worker, because the worker set
 3743	        # that event itself and the wait would return at once.
 3744	        self._pause = threading.Event()
 3745	
 3746	    # -- the lifecycle ----------------------------------------------------
 3747	    def run(self):
 3748	        try:
 3749	            self.thread_handle = self.api.duplicate_current_thread_handle()
 3750	        except BrokerError as error:
 3751	            self.error = error.code
 3752	            self.done.set()
 3753	            self.ready.set()
 3754	            self.state = 'DONE'
 3755	            self.stopped.set()
 3756	            return
 3757	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 3758	            self.error = type(error).__name__
 3759	            self.done.set()
 3760	            self.ready.set()
 3761	            self.state = 'DONE'
 3762	            self.stopped.set()
 3763	            return
 3764	        self.ready.set()
 3765	        try:
 3766	            while not self._stop_event.is_set():
 3767	                try:
 3768	                    request = self._requests.get(timeout=0.1)
 3769	                except queue.Empty:
 3770	                    continue
 3771	                with self._condition:
 3772	                    generation = (self._generations.popleft()
 3773	                                  if self._generations else self._submitted)
 3774	                self.error = None
 3775	                self.result = None
 3776	                self._error_object = None
 3777	                # The check of the cancellation and the publication of
 3778	                # the intent to enter the operation are **one** atomic
 3779	                # step under the lock of the worker.  A cancellation
 3780	                # that arrives between the check and the operation would
 3781	                # otherwise be answered by a worker that performs the
 3782	                # operation anyway: the cancellation and the operation
 3783	                # would be two uncoordinated facts.
 3784	                with self._condition:
 3785	                    cancelled = self._cancelled
 3786	                    if not cancelled:
 3787	                        self.state = 'IN_IO'
 3788	                        self.inside.set()
 3789	                if cancelled:
 3790	                    # The cancellation reached the worker before the
 3791	                    # operation started: the operation is not performed
 3792	                    # at all and the request is answered as finished.
 3793	                    self._complete(generation)
 3794	                    continue
 3795	                try:
 3796	                    self.result = self.action(request)
 3797	                except BrokerError as error:
 3798	                    self.error = error.code
 3799	                    self._error_object = error
 3800	                except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 3801	                    self.error = type(error).__name__
 3802	                    self._error_object = error
 3803	                finally:
 3804	                    self.inside.clear()
 3805	                    self._complete(generation)
 3806	        finally:
 3807	            self.state = 'STOPPED'
 3808	            self.stopped.set()
 3809	            # The worker is the only owner of the handle of its own
 3810	            # thread and it closes it here, when its thread really ended.
 3811	            self.release_thread_handle()
 3812	
 3813	    def _complete(self, generation=None):
 3814	        """Report the real completion of the current request.
 3815	
 3816	        The outcome of the generation is **recorded** here, under the
 3817	        lock of the worker: the result, the code of the error, the
 3818	        **original error object** and the order of the completion.  The
 3819	        record is the history of the request and it is never replaced
 3820	        by a later one.
 3821	
 3822	        The order of the completion is the order of the events of the
 3823	        worker: it tells whether a failure was published **before** the
 3824	        cancellation of the round or after it.  A failure that was
 3825	        published before the cancellation is a fault of the round and it
 3826	        stays the first error; a failure that was published after the
 3827	        cancellation is the consequence of the cancellation.
 3828	        """
 3829	        with self._condition:
 3830	            self._completed += 1
 3831	            self._sequence += 1
 3832	            if generation is not None:
 3833	                record = {
 3834	                    'generation': generation,
 3835	                    'result': self.result,
 3836	                    'error': self.error,
 3837	                    'error_object': self._error_object,
 3838	                    'source': 'WORKER',
 3839	                    'sequence': self._sequence,
 3840	                    'cancel_sequence': self._cancel_sequence,
 3841	                }
 3842	                self._outcomes[generation] = record
 3843	                if record['error'] is not None:
 3844	                    # The failure of this generation is published: a
 3845	                    # later cancellation of the round may not replace
 3846	                    # it.
 3847	                    self.outcome_published.set()
 3848	            self._condition.notify_all()
 3849	        self.done.set()
 3850	        self.state = 'DONE'
 3851	
 3852	    def outcome(self, generation):
 3853	        """The recorded outcome of one generation of this worker.
 3854	
 3855	        The outcome is the **original** outcome of the request: the
 3856	        result, the error and the error object of the request that
 3857	        really ran.  It is ``None`` when the request did not complete.
 3858	        """
 3859	        with self._condition:
 3860	            record = self._outcomes.get(generation)
 3861	            return dict(record) if record is not None else None
 3862	
 3863	    def published_before_cancel(self, generation):
 3864	        """Whether the outcome of this generation was published first.
 3865	
 3866	        The comparison of the two orders is the only proof that a
 3867	        failure of the round happened **before** the cancellation of the
 3868	        round.  A cancellation that was requested first may not rewrite
 3869	        the failure of a request that was still running, and a failure
 3870	        that was published first may never be replaced by a
 3871	        cancellation.
 3872	
 3873	        A worker that was never cancelled proves the order by itself:
 3874	        the failure was published before any cancellation of it.
 3875	        """
 3876	        with self._condition:
 3877	            record = self._outcomes.get(generation)
 3878	            if record is None or record.get('error') is None:
 3879	                return False
 3880	            if self._cancel_sequence is None:
 3881	                return True
 3882	            return record['sequence'] < self._cancel_sequence
 3883	
 3884	    def _request_cancellation(self):
 3885	        """Take the order of the cancellation of this worker.
 3886	
 3887	        The order is taken under the lock of the worker, so that the
 3888	        comparison with the order of a completion is a real order and
 3889	        not a guess of the scheduler.
 3890	        """
 3891	        with self._condition:
 3892	            self._cancelled = True
 3893	            if self._cancel_sequence is None:
 3894	                self._sequence += 1
 3895	                self._cancel_sequence = self._sequence
 3896	            self.state = 'CANCELLING'
 3897	            self._condition.notify_all()
 3898	        self._stop_event.set()
 3899	
 3900	    def wait_ready(self, seconds):
 3901	        """Wait for the worker to have acquired the handle of its thread."""
 3902	        return self.ready.wait(max(0.0, seconds))
 3903	
 3904	    def submit(self, request):
 3905	        """Hand one request to the worker.
 3906	
 3907	        The hand over is bounded: the queue of the worker holds one
 3908	        request and a request that does not fit is refused with a named
 3909	        error.  A worker that is dead and a worker whose thread handle
 3910	        could not be created refuse the request as well.
 3911	
 3912	        The hand over and the cancellation share **one** lock: a
 3913	        request that is submitted after the cancellation of the worker
 3914	        is refused and it never enters the queue.  The cancellation and
 3915	        the submission are therefore coordinated by the same generation
 3916	        of the worker and a request of an older generation is never
 3917	        taken for the completion of this one.
 3918	
 3919	        The completion of a request is the number of the completed
 3920	        requests and not the event alone: the completion of an older
 3921	        request is never the completion of this one.
 3922	        """
 3923	        if not self.is_alive():
 3924	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_NOT_ALIVE', self.kind)
 3925	        if self.thread_handle is None:
 3926	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_NOT_READY',
 3927	                               '%s:%s' % (self.kind, self.error))
 3928	        with self._condition:
 3929	            if self._cancelled:
 3930	                # The cancellation of this worker arrived first: the
 3931	                # request is refused and it never enters the queue.
 3932	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_CANCELLED', self.kind)
 3933	            self._submitted += 1
 3934	            generation = self._submitted
 3935	            # The generation travels with the request: the worker takes
 3936	            # the request and the generation of **that** request.  The
 3937	            # generation is registered **before** the request is handed
 3938	            # over: a worker that takes the request never finds an
 3939	            # empty list of generations.
 3940	            self._generations.append(generation)
 3941	            try:
 3942	                self._requests.put(request, timeout=POLL_SLICE_SECONDS)
 3943	            except queue.Full:
 3944	                self._submitted -= 1
 3945	                if self._generations and self._generations[-1] == generation:
 3946	                    self._generations.pop()
 3947	                self._condition.notify_all()
 3948	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_QUEUE_FULL', self.kind)
 3949	            self.done.clear()
 3950	            self.state = 'QUEUED'
 3951	            return generation
 3952	
 3953	    def wait_done(self, generation, timeout):
 3954	        """Wait for the completion of one request, bounded."""
 3955	        with self._condition:
 3956	            return self._condition.wait_for(
 3957	                lambda: self._completed >= generation, max(0.0, timeout))
 3958	
 3959	    def request_cancel(self, deadline=None):
 3960	        """Ask for the cancellation of the operation of this worker.
 3961	
 3962	        The request is not a completion.  The caller has to wait for the
 3963	        real completion of the worker and to join it.
 3964	
 3965	        The cancellation stops the worker from accepting a new task
 3966	        **first** and it drives the worker to its end: the worker that
 3967	        is inside an operation is asked to leave it and the worker that
 3968	        waits for a request is asked to stop waiting.  A cancellation
 3969	        that reports ``NOT_FOUND`` is repeated, bounded by the shared
 3970	        deadline, until the worker reports that **this** generation is
 3971	        done or that the thread really stopped: a cancellation that was
 3972	        sent once and dropped is not a cancellation.
 3973	
 3974	        The retry never waits on the stop event of the worker: the
 3975	        worker set that event itself, a moment before, and a wait that
 3976	        returns at once would end the retry after a single
 3977	        ``NOT_FOUND``.  The retry waits on its own pause instead, so
 3978	        that the request of the operating system is really repeated.
 3979	
 3980	        The completion of an **older** generation never releases the
 3981	        retry: the real completion of the request of this generation is
 3982	        the number of the completed requests.
 3983	
 3984	        The deadline of the retry is **recomputed on every round** of
 3985	        the loop: a deadline that was published after the retry started
 3986	        still shortens it, and the pause of the retry is cropped to the
 3987	        time that is really left.  A retry that used the number of the
 3988	        deadline that it found when it started would wait longer than
 3989	        the round allows.
 3990	        """
 3991	        self._request_cancellation()
 3992	        if self.thread_handle is None:
 3993	            return 'NO_HANDLE'
 3994	        # The bound of the retry is fixed **once**: a retry that used a
 3995	        # deadline that moved with every round would never expire.
 3996	        bound = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
 3997	        if bound is None:
 3998	            # The retry of a cancellation is **always** bounded: a
 3999	            # worker that never ends may not hold the caller for ever.
 4000	            # The bound of the package is used only when neither the
 4001	            # caller nor the context of the round brought a deadline.
 4002	            bound = self._clock() + CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS
 4003	        while True:
 4004	            try:
 4005	                state = self.api.cancel_synchronous_io(self.thread_handle)
 4006	            except BrokerError as error:
 4007	                self.error = error.code
 4008	                return 'FAILED'
 4009	            except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 4010	                self.error = type(error).__name__
 4011	                return 'FAILED'
 4012	            if state != 'NOT_FOUND':
 4013	                return state
 4014	            # The operation was not found: it had not started yet or it
 4015	            # had just finished.  The worker is asked again until the
 4016	            # request of this generation completed or until the thread
 4017	            # of the worker really stopped.
 4018	            with self._condition:
 4019	                completed = self._completed >= self._submitted
 4020	            if completed or self.stopped.is_set():
 4021	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
 4022	            # The deadline of this round of the retry is read **again**
 4023	            # from the call and from the context of the round: a
 4024	            # deadline that was published after the retry started still
 4025	            # shortens the retry that is running.
 4026	            effective = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
 4027	            if effective is None or bound < effective:
 4028	                effective = bound
 4029	            if self._clock() >= effective:
 4030	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
 4031	            # The pause of the retry is its own event: the stop event of
 4032	            # the worker was set by this very method and it may never be
 4033	            # used as the sleep of the retry.  The pause is cropped to
 4034	            # the time that is really left.
 4035	            pause = POLL_SLICE_SECONDS
 4036	            if effective is not None:
 4037	                pause = max(0.0, min(pause, effective - self._clock()))
 4038	            if pause <= 0:
 4039	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
 4040	            self._pause.wait(pause)
 4041	
 4042	    def _effective_deadline(self, call=None):
 4043	        """The ONE deadline that a wait of this worker really uses.
 4044	
 4045	        The deadline of the call, the absolute deadline of the round and
 4046	        the **published** cleanup deadline of the round are one value:
 4047	        the earliest of them.  The value is recomputed on every call.
 4048	        """
 4049	        value = _require_deadline(call)
 4050	        context = self._context
 4051	        if context is not None:
 4052	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
 4053	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
 4054	                value = absolute
 4055	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
 4056	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
 4057	                value = cleanup
 4058	        return value
 4059	
 4060	    def stop(self):
 4061	        """Ask the worker to end its loop."""
 4062	        self._stop_event.set()
 4063	
 4064	    def stop_accepting(self):
 4065	        """Stop the worker from accepting a new task.
 4066	
 4067	        The flag is written under the **same** lock that the worker of
 4068	        the endpoint reads it under: the cancellation and the intent to
 4069	        enter the operation are one atomic step.
 4070	
 4071	        The **order** of the cancellation is taken here as well: a
 4072	        failure that was published before this call is a fault of the
 4073	        round and a cancellation may never replace it.
 4074	        """
 4075	        self._request_cancellation()
 4076	
 4077	    def release_thread_handle(self):
 4078	        """Close the handle of the thread of this worker.
 4079	
 4080	        The worker is the only owner of that handle.  It is closed here
 4081	        and only here, and only after the thread of the worker really
 4082	        ended.  A worker that did not stop keeps its handle.
 4083	        """
 4084	        if self.thread_handle is None:
 4085	            return True
 4086	        handle = self.thread_handle
 4087	        try:
 4088	            self.api.close_handle(handle)
 4089	        except BrokerError as error:
 4090	            self.error = error.code
 4091	            return False
 4092	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 4093	            self.error = type(error).__name__
 4094	            return False
 4095	        self.thread_handle = None
 4096	        self.thread_handle_released = True
 4097	        return True
 4098	
 4099	    def join_bounded(self, seconds):
 4100	        """Join the worker and report whether it really stopped.
 4101	
 4102	        A worker whose thread was never started cannot be joined: the
 4103	        join of such a thread is an error of the caller and not a fact
 4104	        of the round.  The join reports that the worker did not stop
 4105	        and it never pretends that the thread ended.
 4106	        """
 4107	        try:
 4108	            self.join(max(0.0, seconds))
 4109	        except RuntimeError:
 4110	            # The thread was never started: it is not a thread of the
 4111	            # round that ended, it is a thread that never came up.
 4112	            self.joined = False
 4113	            return False
 4114	        self.joined = not self.is_alive()
 4115	        return self.joined
 4116	
 4117	
 4118	class _WindowsPipeEndpoint:
 4119	    """One end of a private pipe on a Windows host.
 4120	
 4121	    The read and the write of the endpoint each own exactly one worker
 4122	    thread and there is never more than one of them: the number of the
 4123	    threads of a channel is fixed and it never grows with the number of
 4124	    the frames.
 4125	
 4126	    The supervisor never performs the blocking call itself.  It waits
 4127	    for the completion of the worker in bounded slices, it checks the
 4128	    cancel flag and the deadline on every slice and it cancels the
 4129	    worker through the original handle of its thread when the deadline
 4130	    arrives.  The worker that is inside the call is the one that may
 4131	    block; the supervisor never does.
 4132	
 4133	    The cancellation of a synchronous operation is a request and not a
 4134	    completion: ``CancelSynchronousIo`` may report that the operation
 4135	    was not found because it had not started yet or because it had just
 4136	    finished.  The worker therefore reports the real state of its
 4137	    operation, the supervisor joins it with a bounded wait, and a worker
 4138	    that did not stop keeps its owner reference and makes the round
 4139	    unknown.
 4140	
 4141	    A write is a **complete** write: the endpoint writes the whole
 4142	    block, in as many partial writes as Windows needs, and it reports
 4143	    the number of the bytes that really went into the pipe.  A partial
 4144	    write is never reported as a success.
 4145	    """
 4146	
 4147	    #: The bound absolute deadline of this endpoint.  It is a class
 4148	    #: attribute so that an endpoint that was built without its
 4149	    #: constructor still has a real deadline and never a missing one.
 4150	    _deadline = None
 4151	    #: The cancel flag and the deadline of the write that is in flight.
 4152	    #: They are class attributes for the same reason as the deadline
 4153	    #: above: an endpoint that was built without its constructor still
 4154	    #: has them and never a missing one.
 4155	    _write_cancel = None
 4156	    _write_deadline = None
 4157	    #: The context of the round.  It is a class attribute for the same
 4158	    #: reason: an endpoint that was built without its constructor still
 4159	    #: reads the absolute deadline of the round from it and it never
 4160	    #: treats a missing context as an unlimited budget.
 4161	    _context = None
 4162	
 4163	    def __init__(self, file_object, *, name, api=None, clock=None,
 4164	                 handle=None, context=None):
 4165	        self._file = file_object
 4166	        self._name = name
 4167	        self._api = api
 4168	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
 4169	        self._closed = False
 4170	        self._reader = None
 4171	        self._writer = None
 4172	        self._retained = []
 4173	        self._deadline = None
 4174	        self._write_cancel = None
 4175	        self._write_deadline = None
 4176	        # The lock of the **admission** of the endpoint.  The check of
 4177	        # the cancel flag, of the deadline of the round and the entry of
 4178	        # the request into the worker are one atomic step under it.
 4179	        self._admission_lock = threading.RLock()
 4180	        # The ONE context of the round.  The endpoint reads the absolute
 4181	        # deadline of the round from it and it never keeps a budget of
 4182	        # its own.
 4183	        self._context = context
 4184	        if handle is None:
 4185	            import msvcrt
 4186	            handle = msvcrt.get_osfhandle(file_object.fileno())
 4187	        self._handle = handle
 4188	        self.facts = {'name': name, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_ENDPOINT'}
 4189	
 4190	    def bind_deadline(self, deadline):
 4191	        """Bind an absolute deadline to this endpoint.
 4192	
 4193	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
 4194	        bound and it is read again on every slice.
 4195	        """
 4196	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
 4197	        if value is None:
 4198	            return self._deadline
 4199	        if self._deadline is not None and value > self._deadline:
 4200	            return self._deadline
 4201	        self._deadline = value
 4202	        return value
 4203	
 4204	    def bind_context(self, context):
 4205	        """Bind the ONE context of the round to this endpoint."""
 4206	        if context is not None:
 4207	            self._context = context
 4208	        return self._context
 4209	
 4210	    def _effective_deadline(self, deadline=None):
 4211	        """The deadline that this call really uses.
 4212	
 4213	        The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
 4214	        endpoint, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
 4215	        cleanup deadline of the round are **one** value: the earliest of
 4216	        the four.  The endpoint never keeps a deadline of its own and it
 4217	        never treats a missing context as an unlimited budget.
 4218	
 4219	        The published cleanup deadline is part of it: a round that
 4220	        entered its cleanup publishes one deadline and the endpoint
 4221	        that reads the context afterwards is bound by it.  An endpoint
 4222	        that still waited with the long lease of the round would
 4223	        outlive the only cleanup budget of the round.
 4224	        """
 4225	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
 4226	        bound = self._deadline
 4227	        if bound is not None and (value is None or bound < value):
 4228	            value = bound
 4229	        context = self._context
 4230	        if context is not None:
 4231	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
 4232	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
 4233	                value = absolute
 4234	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
 4235	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
 4236	                value = cleanup
 4237	        return value
 4238	
 4239	    def _admission(self, deadline=None, cancel=None):
 4240	        """The admission of one generation of this endpoint.
 4241	
 4242	        The admission is asked **before** every side effect: before the
 4243	        worker of the endpoint is started, before a request is
 4244	        submitted and before the primitive is called.  A call that
 4245	        entered when the round was already expired or already cancelled
 4246	        is refused there: it never starts a worker, it never submits a
 4247	        request and it never calls the primitive.
 4248	
 4249	        The check and the admission of the generation are **one atomic
 4250	        step** under the lock of the endpoint: a cancellation that
 4251	        arrives between the check and the submit may not be answered by
 4252	        a request that was submitted anyway.
 4253	        """
 4254	        effective = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
 4255	        with self._admission_lock:
 4256	            if cancel is not None and cancel.is_set():
 4257	                # The cancellation of the round arrived first: the
 4258	                # request is refused and no side effect happens.
 4259	                raise _cancel_source_error(
 4260	                    BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name), cancel)
 4261	            context = self._context
 4262	            if context is not None and context.cancel.is_set():
 4263	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name)
 4264	            if effective is not None and self._clock() >= effective:
 4265	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self._name)
 4266	            return effective
 4267	
 4268	    def _remaining(self, deadline=None):
 4269	        """The seconds that are left until the deadline of this endpoint."""
 4270	        value = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
 4271	        if value is None:
 4272	            return CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS
 4273	        return max(0.0, value - self._clock())
 4274	
 4275	    def _start(self, kind, action, deadline=None):
 4276	        """Create and start one worker of this endpoint.
 4277	
 4278	        The worker is registered with the endpoint **before** it is
 4279	        started.  An endpoint that owns a worker only after the start
 4280	        returned has no owner at all for a worker whose start did not
 4281	        return, and the close of the endpoint could close the file
 4282	        under a worker that is about to read from it.
 4283	
 4284	        The wait for the handle of the thread of the worker is bounded
 4285	        by the **current** remaining budget of the round.  It never
 4286	        opens a budget of its own and it never waits a fixed number of
 4287	        seconds.
 4288	        """
 4289	        worker = _WindowsPipeWorker(self._api, self._handle, kind=kind,
 4290	                                    action=action, clock=self._clock,
 4291	                                    context=self._context,
 4292	                                    name='lvw-native-custody-pipe-' + kind)
 4293	        # The pre-registration: the endpoint is the owner of the worker
 4294	        # before the thread of the worker exists.
 4295	        if kind == 'read':
 4296	            self._reader = worker
 4297	        else:
 4298	            self._writer = worker
 4299	        try:
 4300	            worker.start()
 4301	        except BaseException:
 4302	            # The start of the worker did not return.  The worker keeps
 4303	            # its owner and it may still come up: it is never forgotten
 4304	            # and it is never closed under.
 4305	            self._retained.append(worker)
 4306	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = len(self._retained)
 4307	            self.facts['worker_alive'] = True
 4308	            raise
 4309	        # The worker acquires the handle of its own thread before it may
 4310	        # be used.  The wait for it is bounded by the remaining budget of
 4311	        # the round.
 4312	        worker.wait_ready(self._remaining(deadline))
 4313	        return worker
 4314	
 4315	    def _await(self, worker, request, deadline, cancel):
 4316	        """Wait for the completion of one worker, bounded and cancellable.
 4317	
 4318	        The deadline that this wait really uses is **recomputed on every
 4319	        slice**: a deadline that was bound after the wait started still
 4320	        tightens the wait that is running.  A wait that carries no
 4321	        deadline yet observes the deadline as soon as it is bound, and
 4322	        the cancel flag ends it in the meantime.
 4323	
 4324	        The **result of the generation** wins over the cancellation.
 4325	        A fault that the worker really published before the
 4326	        cancellation of the round is the first error of the round: the
 4327	        cancellation of a later close may not replace it and it may not
 4328	        turn it into a benign cancellation.  The order of the two facts
 4329	        is the order of the events of the worker and it is never a guess
 4330	        of the scheduler.
 4331	        """
 4332	        generation = worker.submit(request)
 4333	        while True:
 4334	            effective = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
 4335	            if cancel is not None and cancel.is_set():
 4336	                self._raise_cancellation(worker, generation, effective, cancel)
 4337	            if effective is not None and self._clock() >= effective:
 4338	                self._cancel(worker, effective)
 4339	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self._name)
 4340	            if worker.wait_done(generation, POLL_SLICE_SECONDS):
 4341	                break
 4342	        outcome = worker.outcome(generation)
 4343	        if outcome is not None and outcome.get('error') is not None:
 4344	            raise self._outcome_error(outcome)
 4345	        if worker.error:
 4346	            raise BrokerError(worker.error, self._name)
 4347	        return worker.result
 4348	
 4349	    @staticmethod
 4350	    def _outcome_error(outcome):
 4351	        """The error of one published outcome of a worker.
 4352	
 4353	        The **original** error object is raised when the worker kept
 4354	        it: the object is the failure and the code is only its name.  A
 4355	        worker that kept only a code raises the stable code of the
 4356	        broker.
 4357	        """
 4358	        original = outcome.get('error_object')
 4359	        if original is not None:
 4360	            return original
 4361	        return BrokerError(outcome.get('error'), 'WORKER_OUTCOME')
 4362	
 4363	    def _raise_cancellation(self, worker, generation, effective, cancel):
 4364	        """Raise the cancellation of this generation of the worker.
 4365	
 4366	        The outcome of the generation is asked **before** the worker is
 4367	        cancelled and it is asked again after the worker really stopped.
 4368	        A failure of this generation is the **first error** of the
 4369	        round: a cancellation that arrives afterwards may never replace
 4370	        it and it may never turn it into a benign cancellation.  The
 4371	        cancellation stays a **parallel** fact and it is named next to
 4372	        the failure.
 4373	
 4374	        The error of the cancellation carries the owner of the flag: the
 4375	        origin of the cancellation is named at the place where the error
 4376	        is really raised and never by a caller of it.
 4377	        """
 4378	        outcome = worker.outcome(generation)
 4379	        if outcome is not None and outcome.get('error') is not None:
 4380	            raise self._parallel_fault(outcome, cancel)
 4381	        self._cancel(worker, effective)
 4382	        outcome = worker.outcome(generation)
 4383	        if outcome is not None and outcome.get('error') is not None:
 4384	            raise self._parallel_fault(outcome, cancel)
 4385	        raise _cancel_source_error(
 4386	            BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name), cancel)
 4387	
 4388	    @staticmethod
 4389	    def _parallel_fault(outcome, cancel):
 4390	        """The failure of a generation with the cancellation beside it.
 4391	
 4392	        The failure is the error of the round.  The cancellation that
 4393	        was requested at the same moment is a **parallel** fact: it is
 4394	        named next to the failure and it never replaces it.
 4395	        """
 4396	        error = _WindowsPipeEndpoint._outcome_error(outcome)
 4397	        if cancel is not None and cancel.is_set():
 4398	            source = None
 4399	            owner = getattr(cancel, 'owner', None)
 4400	            if callable(owner):
 4401	                try:
 4402	                    source = getattr(owner(), 'owner', None)
 4403	                except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 4404	                    source = None
 4405	            try:
 4406	                error.lvw_parallel_cancellation = {
 4407	                    'code': 'BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED',
 4408	                    'source': source,
 4409	                    'generation': outcome.get('generation'),
 4410	                    'sequence': outcome.get('sequence'),
 4411	                    'cancel_sequence': outcome.get('cancel_sequence'),
 4412	                }
 4413	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
 4414	                pass
 4415	        return error
 4416	
 4417	    def _cancel(self, worker, deadline=None):
 4418	        """Cancel one worker and prove that it really stopped."""
 4419	        state = worker.request_cancel(self._effective_deadline(deadline))
 4420	        if not worker.join_bounded(self._remaining(deadline)):
 4421	            # The worker did not stop: it keeps its owner reference and
 4422	            # the round becomes unknown.  It is never terminated blindly
 4423	            # and it is never waited for without a bound.
 4424	            self._retained.append(worker)
 4425	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = len(self._retained)
 4426	            self.facts['worker_alive'] = True
 4427	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCEL_NOT_CONFIRMED',
 4428	                              '%s:%s' % (self._name, state))
 4429	        if not worker.joined:
 4430	            self._retained.append(worker)
 4431	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = len(self._retained)
 4432	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCEL_NOT_CONFIRMED', self._name)
 4433	
 4434	    def read(self, deadline=None, cancel=None):
 4435	        """Read one block of the pipe, bounded by the deadline.
 4436	
 4437	        The admission of the generation is asked **before** the worker is
 4438	        started and before the request is submitted: a read that enters
 4439	        when the round is already expired or already cancelled performs
 4440	        **no** primitive call at all.
 4441	        """
 4442	        effective = self._admission(deadline, cancel)
 4443	        if self._reader is None:
 4444	            self._start('read', self._read_block, effective)
 4445	        return self._await(self._reader, None, deadline, cancel)
 4446	
 4447	    def write(self, data, deadline=None, cancel=None):
 4448	        """Write the whole block, bounded by the deadline.
 4449	
 4450	        The admission of the generation is asked **before** the worker is
 4451	        started and before the request is submitted: a write that enters
 4452	        when the round is already expired or already cancelled performs
 4453	        **no** primitive call at all.
 4454	        """
 4455	        effective = self._admission(deadline, cancel)
 4456	        if self._writer is None:
 4457	            self._start('write', self._write_block, effective)
 4458	        # The cancel flag and the deadline of this write are published
 4459	        # for the partial write: every segment of a partial write checks
 4460	        # the cancel flag and the deadline of the round.  They are not
 4461	        # cleared when the write ends: a segment that is still in flight
 4462	        # inside the worker has to observe the cancel of this write.
 4463	        self._write_cancel = cancel
 4464	        self._write_deadline = effective
 4465	        return self._await(self._writer, bytes(data), deadline, cancel)
 4466	
 4467	    def _read_block(self, request):
 4468	        return self._api.read_stream(self._handle, PIPE_READ_BYTES)
 4469	
 4470	    def _current_write_deadline(self):
 4471	        """The deadline that the next segment of a write really uses.
 4472	
 4473	        The value is read **again** for every segment of a partial
 4474	        write.  A copy of the deadline that was taken when the write
 4475	        started would let the write continue past a deadline that the
 4476	        round published in the meantime, and the shared context of the
 4477	        round is the only authority of the deadline.
 4478	
 4479	        The **published cleanup deadline** is part of it: a write that
 4480	        is still running when the round entered its cleanup is bounded by
 4481	        the cleanup budget of the round and not by the long lease.
 4482	        """
 4483	        value = self._write_deadline
 4484	        bound = self._deadline
 4485	        if bound is not None and (value is None or bound < value):
 4486	            value = bound
 4487	        context = self._context
 4488	        if context is not None:
 4489	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
 4490	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
 4491	                value = absolute
 4492	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
 4493	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
 4494	                value = cleanup
 4495	        return value
 4496	
 4497	    def _write_block(self, request):
 4498	        """Write the whole block, report the number of the written bytes.
 4499	
 4500	        Windows may write less than the block that it was given.  The
 4501	        endpoint repeats the write until the whole block was written or
 4502	        until the write fails with a named error.  **Every segment** of
 4503	        the partial write checks the cancel flag and the deadline of the
 4504	        round: a write that is not bounded is not a write.
 4505	        """
 4506	        data = bytes(request)
 4507	        total = 0
 4508	        while total < len(data):
 4509	            cancel = self._write_cancel
 4510	            if cancel is not None and cancel.is_set():
 4511	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name)
 4512	            deadline = self._current_write_deadline()
 4513	            if deadline is not None and self._clock() >= deadline:
 4514	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self._name)
 4515	            count = self._api.write_stream(self._handle, data[total:])
 4516	            if count is None or count <= 0:
 4517	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_STREAM_WRITE_FAILED',
 4518	                                  '%s:written=%d' % (self._name, total))
 4519	            total += count
 4520	        return total
 4521	
 4522	    def close(self):
 4523	        """Close the original handle of this endpoint, exactly once.
 4524	
 4525	        The close stops the workers from accepting a new task first, it
 4526	        asks for the cancellation of the operation that is in flight and
 4527	        it joins the workers with a bounded wait.  A worker that did not
 4528	        stop keeps its owner reference: the file is never closed under a
 4529	        worker that is still inside its operation.
 4530	
 4531	        The close is **committed** only when it really happened.  A
 4532	        close that failed keeps the file and its owner: the endpoint
 4533	        may try it again inside the deadline of the round and it is
 4534	        never marked as closed irreversibly.  A close of an endpoint
 4535	        that is already closed keeps the fact that it is closed: the
 4536	        second close is idempotent and it never turns a proven close
 4537	        into a missing one.
 4538	
 4539	        The state of the workers is **recomputed** on every close: a
 4540	        worker that really ended and whose handle of its thread was
